The page and the runner stay in every app and refuse outside the smoke app. Hermes bytecode of `index.android.bundle` in the release APK, x86_64: `c75c31491` 5,267,324 bytes; the product 5,284,588 (+0.33 %); the QA app 5,284,600 (+0.33 %); the smoke app 6,918,312 (+31 %, as the QA app was before the stubs: 6,918,928). `qa/test/bundle.test.ts` bundles the three variants the way React Native's Gradle plugin does (`expo export:embed`, release, not minified; a cache of its own, because Metro's transform cache is not keyed by `NODE_ENV` and a bundle made after a `bun test` run, which sets `test`, carries Reanimated's debug information): in the product and the QA app no package name is in the bundle outside a stub and none of the libraries' code is, the QA bundle is within 1 % of the size `c75c31491` has with that recipe (10,944,737 bytes of JavaScript; 10,963,530 then, +0.17 %) and the product's within 1 % of its own reference (the two were equal to 0.1 % until the product left out the lab, see "What the product contains"), and the smoke app's has the libraries.

`qa/test/smoke.test.ts` evaluates `app.config.ts` for the three variants and runs both autolinkers with the excludes the plugin gets, for Android and iOS: the product's and the QA app's output has none of the packages and is the same set, package for package; the smoke app's is that set plus the smoke-only packages with code for the platform, no more. The generated files of real builds confirm it (`PackageList.java` from `:app:generateAutolinkingPackageList`, `ExpoModulesPackageList.kt` from `:expo:generatePackagesList`, after `expo prebuild` of each variant, and the same two for a clean checkout of `c75c31491`):

| | `PackageList.java` | `ExpoModulesPackageList.kt` | `settings.gradle` excludes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `c75c31491` | 7 packages | 21 modules | 12 (headless) |
| product | 7, identical | 21, identical | 12 + 31 |
| QA app | 7, identical to `c75c31491` | 21, identical to `c75c31491` | 12 + 31 |
| smoke app | 25 | 43 | 12 |

The seven packages are `MainReactPackage`, `ExpoModulesPackage`, hermes-intl, react-native-maps, Reanimated, react-native-webview and Worklets; the twenty-one modules are application, asset, constants, crypto and its AES module, EAS client, fetch, file system and its legacy module, the two font modules, image picker, keep awake, localization, secure store, splash screen, the engine, the updates module, system UI, video and view bench. On iOS the QA app's and the product's `Podfile` carry `use_expo_modules!(exclude: svelte_native_exclude)` and the `--exclude` extension of the config command, no `ios.useFrameworks` and no Firebase call in the AppDelegate; the smoke app's has `FirebaseApp.configure()`, the fake `GoogleService-Info.plist` and static frameworks (prebuild only: no pods were installed).

`native-qa build --variant smoke` builds the smoke app (`qa/out/app-smoke-<abi>.apk`, `build-smoke-<abi>.json`, `.log`); the default is the QA app, whatever `APP_VARIANT` the shell has. `android/` holds one variant at a time, and only a finished project counts: `android/.native-qa-variant` (written when a prebuild ends) says which, and a project without it (a prebuild that failed or was killed, one made by hand) is of unknown origin, never assembled as the QA app and never parked. A prebuild writes `android.prebuilding` when it starts and removes it when it ends; a failed one removes the half project, one that was killed leaves the file and the next build removes the half project. Building the other variant renames the project to `android-<variant>/` and brings the other back, or prebuilds it when none is parked, so a failed smoke prebuild never costs the parked QA project. The generated project is also made again when any prebuild input is newer (`app.config.ts`, `package.json`, `react-native.config.js`, `qa/smoke-modules.json`, `qa/smoke-packages.cjs`, the fake Firebase file, the Svelte native plugin, the local modules). The iOS project on the Mac gets the same care without a parked copy: `ios/` is generated and shared by the variants (and the smoke app writes `ios.useFrameworks: "static"` into it), so a prebuild for another variant, or after one that did not finish, runs with `--clean` (`qa/lib/ios-project.ts`; the marker `ios/.native-qa-variant` is removed before the prebuild and written after it). The build sets `SENTRY_DISABLE_AUTO_UPLOAD`. `expo-samsung-pay` does not compile without the proprietary Samsung Pay SDK: copy `libs/samsungpay.jar` of the production app to `apps/platform/libs/` (ignored by git); only the smoke build needs it and says so when it is missing. A fresh checkout or CI builds the QA app without it.

`native-qa smoke` installs the smoke APK, launches `rasheed-svelte-smoke:///__dev?run=smoke` in `io.rasheed.svelteqa.smoke` and writes its own run kind (`smoke-<device>-<stamp>.json`); the QA app is not installed or started. The smoke gates are a separate lane, with their own warnings (the report's `smokeWarnings`; the smoke run is another app, so it is not compared with the QA runs' builds, and neither lane fails on the other's): `native-qa gates` judges every gate but `smoke.*` (the QA app), `native-qa gates --smoke` only the six smoke gates: `smoke.complete >= 1` (the run logged its completion line, was not interrupted and did not crash), `smoke.fatals <= 0`, `smoke.failed <= 0`, `smoke.missing <= 0`, `smoke.unexpectedSkips <= 0` (a skip the platform does not list in `smoke.skips` of `gates.json`: Android `hms` and `web-browser`, iOS `web-browser`, `hms` and `samsung-pay`) and `smoke.ok >= N` (the pinned modules less those skips: 34 on Android, 33 on iOS). `native-qa smoke` itself exits 1 when one of these fails, with the gate named, so a script that only runs `smoke` cannot pass a run the lane would fail; `native-qa:emulator` does not run the smoke test, `native-qa:smoke` is its explicit lane (build the smoke app, run it, report, `gates --smoke`).

### What each module needed

The Android and iOS columns are the results each check is written to give, from the libraries' sources; no device has run the test yet, so none of them is observed. A device run replaces them with what happened.

| module | package (installed; specifier) | path | call | Android (expected) | iOS (expected) | needed, and notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `async-storage` | `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` 2.2.0 | js | `setItem`, `getItem`, `removeItem` of one key | ok | ok | nothing |
| `mmkv` | `react-native-mmkv` 4.3.1 | js | `createMMKV`, `set`, `getString`, `remove` | ok | ok | a Nitro hybrid object; needs `react-native-nitro-modules` |
| `secure-store` | `expo-secure-store` 57.0.4 (shared) | js | `setItemAsync`, `getItemAsync`, `deleteItemAsync` | ok | ok | already in the product |
| `netinfo` | `@react-native-community/netinfo` 12.0.1 | js | `configure({ reachabilityShouldRun: () => false })`, `fetch()` | ok | ok | the default configuration probes `clients3.google.com` with a `fetch`; off, `fetch()` reads only the platform's connection state |
| `device-info` | `react-native-device-info` 15.0.2 | js | `getVersion`, `getModel`, `getBundleId`, `getDeviceName` | ok | ok | nothing |
| `expo-device` | `expo-device` 57.0.2 | js | `modelName`, `osName`, `isDevice`, `getDeviceTypeAsync` | ok | ok | nothing |
| `get-random-values` | `react-native-get-random-values` 2.0.0 (`~2.0.0`, nested under `apps/platform`: another package pins 1.11.0) | js+direct | the import installs `crypto.getRandomValues`, filled for a `Uint8Array(16)`; `RNGetRandomValues.getRandomBase64(16)` by name | ok | ok | 2.x's JavaScript prefers `ExpoCrypto` when `expo` is present, so its own API alone would not reach its module; the module is called by name |
| `quick-crypto` | `react-native-quick-crypto` 1.1.3 | js | `randomBytes(16)`, `createHash("sha256")` of `abc` compared with `ba7816bf…15ad` | ok | ok | plugin `{ sodiumEnabled: true }` as in production (libsodium on both platforms); needs Nitro, `@craftzdog/react-native-buffer`, `readable-stream` |
| `quick-base64` | `react-native-quick-base64` 2.2.2 | js | `fromByteArray` of `Svelte Native` = `U3ZlbHRlIE5hdGl2ZQ==`, `toByteArray`, `atob(btoa())` | ok | ok | nothing |
| `nitro` | `react-native-nitro-modules` 0.35.10 (`^0.35.6`) | js | `NitroModules.version`, `buildType`, `getAllHybridObjectNames()` | ok | ok | nothing; MMKV and quick-crypto register their objects |
| `launch-arguments` | `react-native-launch-arguments` 4.1.1 | js | `LaunchArguments.value()` | ok | ok | nothing (an empty object without launch extras) |
| `localization` | `expo-localization` 57.0.2 (shared) | js | `getLocales()`, `getCalendars()` | ok | ok | already in the product |
| `constants` | `expo-constants` 57.0.22 (shared) | js | `Constants.expoConfig` (`slug`, `name`, `version`) | ok | ok | already in the product |
| `asset` | `expo-asset` 57.0.20 (shared) | js | `Asset.fromURI` (no download); module `ExpoAsset` registered | ok | ok | already in the product (a dependency of `expo`) |
| `haptics` | `expo-haptics` 57.0.3 | js | `impactAsync(Light)` | ok | ok | nothing |
| `screen-orientation` | `expo-screen-orientation` 57.0.3 | js | `getOrientationAsync()` | ok | ok | plugin without options (the app is not locked to portrait, unlike production's `initialOrientation`) |
| `store-review` | `expo-store-review` 57.0.3 | js | `isAvailableAsync()` | ok | ok | `requestReview` is not called |
| `system-ui` | `expo-system-ui` 57.0.4 (shared) | js | `getBackgroundColorAsync()`, `setBackgroundColorAsync(same color)` | ok | ok | already in the product; set to what it already is |
| `tracking-transparency` | `expo-tracking-transparency` 57.0.2 | js | `getTrackingPermissionsAsync()` | ok | ok | plugin with a usage text; reads the state, never asks |
| `local-authentication` | `expo-local-authentication` 57.0.3 | js | `hasHardwareAsync()` | ok | ok | `authenticateAsync` is not called |
| `notifications` | `expo-notifications` 57.0.24 | js | `getPermissionsAsync()` | ok | ok | plugin without options (production's icon and colour need its assets); no token is fetched |
| `location` | `expo-location` 57.0.22 | js | `getForegroundPermissionsAsync()` | ok | ok | plugin with the foreground service off (production enables it for one variant); no fix is requested |
| `sharing` | `expo-sharing` 57.0.24 | js | `isAvailableAsync()` | ok | ok | `shareAsync` is not called |
| `web-browser` | `expo-web-browser` 57.0.3 | js | Android: `getCustomTabsSupportingBrowsersAsync()`, `warmUpAsync()`, `coolDownAsync()` | ok, or skip when no browser has Custom Tabs | skip (Android only) | warm-up binds the browser's Custom Tabs service; no tab opens |
| `splash-screen` | `expo-splash-screen` 57.0.10 (shared) | js | `hideAsync()` | ok | ok | already in the product; the engine already hid it after the first commit |
| `ssl-pinning` | `react-native-ssl-public-key-pinning` 1.2.6 | js | `isSslPinningAvailable()` | ok | ok | `initializeSslPinning` is not called (it would change React Native's HTTP client) |
| `firebase` | `@react-native-firebase/{app,analytics,messaging}` 24.1.1 | js | `getApp().name`, `setAnalyticsCollectionEnabled(false)`, `isDeviceRegisteredForRemoteMessages()` | ok | ok | see "Firebase" below |
| `sentry` | `@sentry/react-native` 7.11.0 | direct | `RNSentry.initNativeSdk` (dummy DSN, `enabled: false`), `fetchNativeRelease`, `captureEnvelope` of a message | ok | ok | see "Sentry" below |
| `hms` | `@hmscore/react-native-hms-location` 6.16.0-302 (`^6.16.0-301`; production's lock has `-301-beta1`) | js+direct | loads the library, reads which `HMS*` native modules are registered | skip on a device that is not Huawei or Honor (the library is linked, HMS Core is not there to call); ok on one that is (the native modules are registered; no HMS call is made); fail when no HMS native module is registered | skip (Android only) | Huawei's Maven repository through `expo-build-properties` (`extraMavenRepos`); production's `@armanatz/expo-hms-location` plugin needs a real `agconnect-services.json` and is not applied |
| `samsung-pay` | `expo-samsung-pay` 0.1.18 | js | loads the package; module `ExpoSamsungPay` registered (`canMakePayments` is not called) | ok | skip (Android only) | plugin `{ aarPath: "./libs/samsungpay.jar" }`; the proprietary SDK jar is copied from the production app and ignored by git |
| `intercom` | `@intercom/intercom-react-native` 10.7.0 | js+direct | loads the library; the native module's constants (`IntercomEvents`) answered | ok | ok | plugin with dummy keys and `useManualInit: true`; `initialize` is not called (see below) |
| `statsig` | `@statsig/react-native-bindings` 3.33.7 | js | `StatsigClientRN` with `preventAllNetworkTraffic`, `initializeSync()`, `checkGate()` | ok | ok | no native code of its own; uses async storage and the device libraries |
| `mixpanel` | `mixpanel-react-native` 3.8.1 | js | `init(true)` (opted out), `hasOptedOutTracking()`, `getDistinctId()` | ok | ok | checks the native module is registered (the library falls back to JavaScript without it) |
| `adjust` | `react-native-adjust` 5.8.0 | js | `Adjust.getSdkVersion()` | ok | ok | plugin `@config-plugins/react-native-adjust` as in production; `initSdk` is not called (it sends the install) |
| `check-installed-apps` | `expo-check-installed-apps` 0.2.14 | js | `checkInstalledApps(["com.android.vending", "com.huawei.appmarket"])`; iOS `["mailto", "https"]` | ok | ok | plugin with the same package names as production |
| `live-direction` | `react-native-live-direction` 0.1.1 | js | `require("react-native-live-direction/auto")` (what the production app loads), `getDirection()`, `I18nManager.subscribe` installed | ok | ok | the production app's pnpm patch (`patches/react-native-live-direction@0.1.1.patch`); plugin `{ locales: ["ar", "en"] }` |

**Firebase.** The Firebase files linked into this checkout (the SDK examples' `google-services.json` and `GoogleService-Info.plist`) and the one in the secrets directory do not cover `io.rasheed.svelteqa` or `io.rasheed.svelteqa.smoke` (checked by looking for the ids, nothing printed). The smoke app therefore carries `qa/firebase-fake/google-services.json` and `GoogleService-Info.plist`: well-formed, `io.rasheed.svelteqa.smoke`, project `svelte-native-smoke-fake`, every key fake, so they are committed. `apps/platform/firebase.json` turns off data collection by default, analytics collection (hard-deactivated) and messaging auto-init, and the iOS auto-registration for remote messages, which should keep the app from fetching an installation id or a token; no device has shown that. The three libraries should load, `getApp()` should answer from the native constants, and the two offline calls resolve.

**Sentry.** The Expo plugin `@sentry/react-native/expo` is not applied: it only adds the source map and debug-symbol upload step (`sentry.gradle`, the Xcode build phases), and `SENTRY_DISABLE_AUTO_UPLOAD` is set in the build anyway. The JavaScript client needs React-side integrations, so the check calls the native half it drives: `initNativeSdk` with a dummy DSN on `sentry.invalid` and `enabled: false` (a disabled SDK has no transport, nothing leaves the device), `fetchNativeRelease` (answered by the native module, the package id and version), and `captureEnvelope` with a message event (resolves, and is dropped by the disabled SDK).

**Intercom, Statsig, Mixpanel, Adjust.** Statsig and Mixpanel initialize with dummy keys because both can be made silent: Statsig's `networkConfig.preventAllNetworkTraffic` with logging disabled, Mixpanel opted out from the start. Intercom's and Adjust's SDKs send on initialization (a session or install request) and have no switch for it, so they are loaded and called with a pure call: Intercom's native constants, Adjust's `getSdkVersion`. The Intercom plugin is applied with `useManualInit: true`, so the SDK is not initialized at launch either.

### Platform notes and limits

- iOS: the Podfile edit and the plugin options are written for the Podfile Expo's template generates (a test pins the shape), but nothing was compiled or run on iOS. The smoke app is another Xcode project (`Sveltesmoke`), so `build --platform ios --variant smoke` needs its own GUI Terminal script on the Mac (default `/tmp/rasheed-ios-smoke.command`, made like the lab one for that project). Firebase's pods need static frameworks, so the smoke app sets `ios.useFrameworks = "static"` through `expo-build-properties` (the production app does the same); how the engine's local pods build that way is untested. The iOS run reads `Documents/smoke-results.json`.
- Boot and every other QA gate: unchanged, the QA app has the native modules it had at `c75c31491` (see the table above). The smoke app is heavier by design (31 more packages, Firebase's init provider, 13 more Expo modules registered eagerly: they are not in the lazy list of `patches/expo-modules-core@57.0.23.patch`) and is never measured by a gate other than `smoke.*`.
- The libraries' JavaScript runs on the real `react-native` index (its exports are lazy getters), not the lite shim the engine uses for Expo's own packages. A library that reads a component at import time would fail its check; that is a finding.
- Not verified: nothing was run on a device. What was: the Android builds of the QA app and the smoke app (`:app:assembleRelease`, no `e:` line in either), the type check of every check against the libraries' own types, the harness against logcat text, and the autolinker output.

## Android wave 4 (26-27 October 2026, OVH emulator)

Measured on `localhost:5555` (x86_64, API 36, software GL), release builds, the harness commands of `apps/platform/qa`. Frame numbers on this emulator are not the bar; the results below are correctness, memory and JavaScript time.

### Native heap growth of the soak: cause and fix

**Before.** The 10-minute soak (all parts) grew the native heap (`Native Heap` Heap Alloc, the live malloc bytes) by +7.9 % (the evening's number) and +9.46 % with the idle windows below: JS heap +0.07 %, worklet runtime -0.04 %, 0 leaked views, 0 leaked emitters, 0 dead-tag writes. The floors of the idle windows (baseline, a checkpoint every two minutes, final) rose 58.6, 58.6, 59.3, 61.5, 63.6, 64.1 MB, 647 KB/min: a leak, not an allocator plateau. The 3-minute churn-only soak, the same build, gave +3.1 % and +3.6 % without a Java collection in the windows and +8.4 %, +8.4 %, +6.1 % with one, so the earlier "+5.4-5.9 %" was not a stable number: it moved by five points with whether the Java heap had been collected before the windows (unexplained: the collection lowered the baseline floor by 0.4 MB and raised the final one by 2.3 MB, the leak below was running in both). The windows now collect it, so that no window depends on when ART last ran.

**Measurement changes** (`apps/platform/qa/soak`, `Stress.svelte`): the idle windows collect this runtime, the worklet runtime (`gc()` on the UI thread, which left 14 MB of garbage and 463 KB live) and the Java heap (`SvelteEngine.collectJava()`), and the lab takes a checkpoint window every two minutes; `memory.native.slopeKbPerMin`, `memory.ui.growthPct` and `memory.leakedEmitters` are new metrics ("Stats for the soak runner"). The C++ delegate's tables were not the leak: `emitters` equalled `alive` (100 and 100) at every window.

**Attribution.** `heapprofd` cannot unwind this app's JavaScript thread; `malloc_debug` with backtraces can (`qa/soak/malloc-debug-soak.sh`, `qa/soak/heapdiff.py`, `qa/README.md` "Where a native growth comes from"). Heap dumps at the baseline window, the first checkpoint and the final window of a 7-minute soak: live allocations of 53.1 MB at the baseline and 52.3 MB at the end, so the leak is a few MB inside a heap that is otherwise stable, and one family of sites accounts for it, with the same live counts (about 1,300 more at the end than at the baseline, 1,696 / 3,392 in absolute terms): 537 KB at a Hermes host function in `libreactnative` that creates the nodes (416, 104 and 88 bytes each), 294 KB + 290 KB (112 bytes each) and 124 KB (a `folly::dynamic`'s hash storage, 768 bytes each) under `libreanimated.so!flush` <- `flushUpdates` <- `performOperations`, 115 KB at `UpdatesRegistry`'s `operator[]`. Those are the entries of Reanimated's `UpdatesRegistry::updatesRegistry_` (the last animated props of a node, with its `ShadowNodeFamily`).

**Cause.** `UpdatesRegistryManager::handleNodeRemovals` empties a node's entry once the node was marked removable (`markNodeAsRemovable`, called by the host in `detachAnimation`) and a mount finds it gone from the tree. A mapper's batch that was collected before the node left is flushed into the registry one frame later (`UpdatesRegistry::flush` -> `flushUpdatesToRegistry`, from `performOperations`), after the entry was emptied, and puts it back; nothing marks the node again, so the entry, its props and the family stay for the life of the process: about 1.4 KB for each removed tile that had an update pending, roughly one tile in ten in the churn scenario (the engine's `lateWrites` count the same race on the view side: 0.5 per deleted tile). It is Reanimated's race, present with React as well; the sink (`AnimatedBridge`) and the engine's registries do not take part.

**Fix.** `fabric/animation.ts`: `detachAnimation` marks the node removable now and once more 400 ms later (one shared timer; the next mount, which the soak and every app have within a frame, removes the late entry). A fix in the Reanimated patch (a tombstone of removed tags checked by `flushUpdatesToRegistry`) would stop the race at its source and is the better upstream change; this one needs no change to the patch and no assumption beyond "a mount follows".

**After.** 3-minute churn soak (same two runs): native growth -3.8 %, -3.9 % (`memory.native.slopeKbPerMin` -950, -946: the baseline window, taken after two warm-up cycles, holds the warm-up's allocations); 7-minute malloc dumps: the largest growing site 58 KB (537 KB before), the others below 50 KB (minikin's layout cache, Hermes); live 53.0 MB at the baseline, 50.1 MB at the end. 10-minute soak, all parts: `memory.heapGrowthPct` **0.08** (JS +0.08 %, worklet runtime -0.04 %, native -4.68 %), floors 59.4, 57.1, 55.7, 56.0, 56.2, 56.6 MB (after the first drop +0.13 MB/min), 0 leaked views, 0 leaked emitters, 0 dead-tag writes, 0 crashes.

**Plateau.** 30-minute soak, all parts, the same build, 116,778 views created and destroyed, checkpoint every two minutes: `memory.heapGrowthPct` **0.14** (JS +0.14 %, worklet runtime -0.04 %, native -4.08 %); the native floors after the first drop (59.3 MB at 0.9 min, 56.3 at 3.1) are 56.1-57.1 MB at all 13 later checkpoints and 56.9 MB at the end, +18 KB/min by least squares from the first checkpoint on (0.5 MB over 27 minutes, inside the checkpoints' own scatter of 0.4 MB); `Native Heap` size 102.4 -> 102.7 MB, Dalvik 5.5 -> 5.3 MB, 102 engine views and 102 emitters alive at every window, 0 leaked, 0 dead-tag writes, 0 crashes. What is left is not growing: the first drop is the warm-up's allocations (first-use caches, early garbage) that the baseline window, taken after two cycles, still holds.

The three changes the evening's number depended on (`reportMount` posted to the handler, `EventBeat.induce`, the accessibility click listener) were not bisected by build: one build's own result varied between +3.1 % and +8.4 % depending on what the windows collected, which is more than any of them could be told apart by; the allocation sites above do not involve them.

### Tab bar and header direction

`View.getLayoutDirection()` answers left to right until the view has been measured while attached, and a screen is measured and laid out detached at the end of its batch (`MountingManager.flushScreens`): the tab bar (`TabBar.onLayout`) kept the left-to-right order of its items on some launches until something else asked for a layout, while the items, resolved later, already drew their badges mirrored. `views/Direction.kt` (`View.isRtlLayout()`) reads `resources.configuration.layoutDirection` instead, which is the same for every view at once; `TabBar`, `TabItemView` and `EngineHeader` use it, the label of a tab item is rebuilt when the direction or the font scale it was built with changed, and on a configuration change. `TabItemView.onRtlPropertiesChanged` (added before) is gone. Measured: `/__dev/screens?mode=tabs`, 10 launches in Arabic and 10 in English, `adb exec-out screencap`, all screenshots of a direction identical (0 differing pixels outside the status bar); before the change 2 of 6 Arabic launches came up unmirrored (24,672 px). `parity --quick --filter screens-tabs` after `--update`: 0 px in all four variants, twice. The English references did not change outside the status bar band (masked): the 1 px "drift" of the shapes was not reproducible in 16 English launches (0 px between all of them), only the direction race was.

### TalkBack and the focus-trap check

TalkBack (`com.google.android.marvin.talkback`) is installed on the AVD (the README said it was not). A probe `AccessibilityService` (a 100-line scratch APK that logs `AccessibilityEvent`s and walks `getWindows()`, installed and removed by the session) ran next to it on the menu, sheet, auto-height sheet, form sheet and modal screens, with TalkBack's notification permission granted so that its dialog did not take the focus. Results, all from the node info a service receives:

- A service with the default flags (81: `flagDefault | flagReportViewIds | flagRetrieveInteractiveWindows`) receives, with a sheet or modal over the menu, 33 nodes, none of them labelled like the menu's buttons; over the plain menu it receives all 16 of those nodes. `uiautomator dump --compressed` is the same set (8 and 18 nodes for the sheet and modal screens against 54 and 75 in the default dump, which lists the covered screen).
- A service that also sets `flagIncludeNotImportantViews` (83) receives 155 and 156 nodes, 16 of them the menu's, every one `isImportantForAccessibility() == false` (the covered screen is `noHideDescendants` for services, `EngineStack.refresh` -> `EngineScreen.setA11yHidden`), so TalkBack's focus rule (visible and important and a screen reader target, actionable or speaking) yields 0 focusable nodes of the covered screen in both configurations.
- TalkBack's own first focus after the window changed went into the sheet's content (a `TextView` of the sheet) and into the modal's title ("Screen 1, Native header"). The injected swipes did not move its focus (see qa/README: gestures cannot be driven reliably), so a full walk of TalkBack's traversal was not recorded.

The engine is right; the oracle was wrong: the suite's 112 "focus leaks" were nodes of the full dump that a screen reader never visits. `qa/lib/a11y.ts` judges the focus trap on the `--compressed` dump, and a sheet titled like the button that opened it ("Form sheet") no longer counts its own title. Frozen dumps of the sheet and modal screens (`qa/test/samples/uia-screens-{sheet,modal}-{full,important}.xml`) pin it: the full dump leaks 7 of 7 labels, the walked tree 0. The other checks read the full dump until the same day's next emulator run, whose findings on the sheet and modal screens (an unlabeled stop, content before the sheet's title, stops out of order) were again the full dump's: it lists the sheet's scrim (`IMPORTANT_FOR_ACCESSIBILITY_NO` since the first commit, absent from the compressed dump) and the covered screen's buttons. Every check now reads the compressed dump.

### Resilience

**iPhone** (1 Nov 2026, `native-qa resilience --platform ios`, `qa/README.md` "Resilience on the iPhone"; the short plan, 26 steps, ran green on the iPhone SE 3): the Android plan as far as an iPhone allows, 126 steps through one XCUITest (`ResilienceUITests.swift`) that interprets a plan written in `qa/lib/iphone/resilience.ts`: rotation x 5, the header screen pushed from the menu and the same restart under the largest (`UICTContentSizeCategoryAccessibilityXXXL`), the smallest and the default Dynamic Type, Arabic and English by launch argument, the appearance where the runner's SDK has `XCUIDevice.appearance`, 100 trips to the background, the backgrounded process ended twice and the app returned to (the navigation restore), a link after the end winning over the restored screens, and a process ended in the foreground restoring nothing. A live change of the text size or the language is not possible on a phone from outside (they are settings of the phone; `devicectl` and XCUITest have no call for them), so those steps end the process and launch it with the override: the restore under that size, and the larger text measured on the first row's height. `-UIPreferredContentSizeCategoryName` is how UI tests set Dynamic Type for one launch; on the iPhone SE 3 the first row's height followed it (largest at least 1.5x, smallest at most 0.97x, default within 5 % of the reference) in the short plan. What is judged: the process, the screens on screen, the phone's crash log; gates `resilience.failures`, `resilience.stepsMissing`, `resilience.skipped` (at most the appearance pair) and `resilience.gateBypassed` for the iPhone as for the emulator.

Android: `resilience --apk <build before these changes>`: 126 steps, 0 failed, the rotation (landscape and portrait x 5), font scale (2.0, 0.85, 1.0), locale, 100 background cycles and the two low-memory rounds included (the evening's fixes: `reportMount` posted off the batch, the overlay following `onHostResume`).

### Windowed list: where the mount to layout goes

Lab scenario `list-300-virtual-profile` (the gate's scenario with the host timing every flush) and a build that logs the C++ delegate's `pullTransaction` (encode and JNI, not kept): the first window (10 rows, 40 created nodes) is: Svelte before the host flush 2-6 ms, host flush 8-15 ms, of which `completeRoot` 4-15 ms (Fabric: Yoga, text measurement, Reanimated's commit hook, the differ), `createNode` 1.0-1.5 ms for 40 nodes, payloads 0.2-0.4 ms, `appendChild` 0.3-0.5 ms, the host's own bookkeeping 1.5-2.7 ms; the engine's delegate (`pullTransaction`: encode 0.1 ms, JNI 0.1 ms) is 2 % of `completeRoot`, and the UI-thread apply 1-3 ms. So the Svelte flush that the lab reports (about 11 ms on the A16) is mostly allocation in the JavaScript host plus Fabric's commit.

JavaScript changes (`renderer.ts`, `fabric/host.ts`, `fabric/view-configs.ts`): attributes are written into the node's own object until a snapshot was handed out (the renderer built a snapshot and copied the attribute object on every write), the host state is a field of `NativeNode` (a WeakMap lookup per flush before), colour conversions are cached (256 entries), the per-node loops (`wantsAnimation`, press handlers) allocate no closures and run no regular expression per key. Measured: V8 profile of 3,000 mounts, garbage collection 24 % before and 1 % after; emulator, release build, the first mount of the list in a fresh process, 10 interleaved pairs of lab runs: the JavaScript part (Svelte plus host flush) median 17.9 ms before, 9.1 ms after (range 10.0-45.7 and 8.1-16.3 ms; Mann-Whitney p < 0.001), mount to layout median 22.5 ms before and 16.6 ms after (p = 0.06 while another run loaded the host); steady state (40 mounts in one process, 4 runs each) median 7.85 ms before and 7.3 ms after. The A16 was not measured here (the bar is 10 ms on it: 12.6-13.9 ms before); `completeRoot` is Fabric's and the remaining lever is outside `host.ts`.


## Gestures (react-native-gesture-handler)

Design written on 28 Oct 2026 (branch `feat/svelte-native-gestures`) from the source of `react-native-gesture-handler` (RNGH) **2.32.0**, the version Expo SDK 57 pins (`~2.32.0`; it is now a direct dependency of `apps/platform`), Reanimated 4.5.1, worklets 0.10.1 and React Native 0.86.3. **The design below is implemented** on both platforms and was measured, between 28 Oct and 1 Nov 2026, in four places; "Test plan" and the status paragraphs of each contract hold the numbers. (1) On the host: `bun test` in `packages/svelte-native` (the golden vectors are RNGH's own detector code captured against a stub of `react-native`, `packages/svelte-native/tests/gestures/golden/`), the Kotlin host checks of `android/src/test` (id and pointer-events rules, who may claim a touch, the lab switches), an esbuild import-graph check of the modules the design reuses, `transformer.ts` on `.svelte` fixtures. (2) On the OVH Android emulator (release x86_64): the lab's `gestures-*` scenarios with `adb input` and with the in-app injector, the 28 lab checks and `parity` with and without the gesture root. (3) On an iPhone SE 3 (iOS 26.6): the same scenarios driven by an XCUITest bundle, the pop gesture and sheet arbitration measured, the lab. (4) `:app:assembleRelease` for the QA variant, and a prebuild of the product variant, which leaves the QA-only modules out. **Not measured:** a Samsung phone, a person's finger on either platform (touches are injected: `adb input`, the in-app injector, XCUITest), TalkBack and VoiceOver on a gesture, the one-hour `gestures --repeat 10` run, and any change made after a status paragraph's date that the paragraph does not list (the Mac has to compile and run the iOS parts of the review fixes of 1 Nov: see "iOS status (lane G5)"). Everything else is read from source, with the file to confirm it in.

### Decisions

| Question | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Native side | RNGH's own native module, orchestrator and recognisers, unchanged, on both platforms. The engine adapts its views to what RNGH expects (Android); iOS needs one patch and one delegate. | RNGH is a production library whose arbitration logic (relations, activation, cancellation) is the thing the user asked for. Re-implementing it is the risk, adapting a View tree is not. |
| JS builders | Reuse `Gesture.Pan()/Tap()/...`, `Gesture.Simultaneous/Exclusive/Race` and their config exactly as they are (`gestureObjects.ts` and the gesture classes). | They are pure JS: 22 RNGH modules plus `import React from "react"` (for `React.version`), no `react-native`, no `createHandler`, no Reanimated (measured with an esbuild metafile). |
| JS detector | Rewrite RNGH's `GestureDetector/*` (attach, update, drop, relations, Reanimated event handler) without React, as a host module that mirrors `fabric/animation.ts`. Golden vectors captured from RNGH's own code pin its output. | `GestureDetector` is a React component, and `GestureDetector/utils.ts` imports `createHandler.tsx`, which patches `UIManager` at module scope; neither can load on this host. |
| Svelte API | A **`gesture` attribute** on host elements and the primitives that forward attributes (`View`, `Pressable`, `ScrollView`, `AnimatedView`, `Image`, `Text`), the way `animatedStyle` and `entering` already work. `<GestureDetector gesture>` (a `View` wrapper) and `use:gesture` are sugar over the same attribute. | `use:` is not allowed on a component (`AnimatedView`, or any primitive the lowering leaves a component), and a draggable box must carry the gesture on the moving view itself, not on a static wrapper. The attribute reaches the host with the node's tag already allocated (`State.tag`), after the commit, which is exactly when native attach needs it. |
| Framework root | The app wraps nothing. Android: the engine's `EngineRoot` hosts RNGH's `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper`, installed lazily on the first gesture. iOS: RNGH adds its root recogniser to the surface view itself at the first attach. | `RNGestureHandlerRootView` is a Fabric component mounted by React Native; the engine's tree is not. |
| UI-thread callbacks | Reanimated worklets, through `WorkletEventHandler` (what `useEvent` wraps), keyed by the node's tag. JS callbacks come through `RCTDeviceEventEmitter`. | Both are RNGH's own native routes; neither needs a Fabric event emitter (see the facts). |

### What the source says

Android (`node_modules/react-native-gesture-handler/android/src/main/java/com/swmansion/gesturehandler/`):
- The registry is keyed by view id: `RNGestureHandlerRegistry.getHandlersForView(view) = handlersForView[view.id]` (react/RNGestureHandlerRegistry.kt:93). The orchestrator walks the `View` tree (`GestureHandlerOrchestrator.extractGestureHandlers`: children topmost first, `left/top/scrollX/scrollY/matrix`, `clipChildren`, `ReactPointerEventsView`, alpha below 0.1 skipped). There is no "view for tag" lookup; `attachGestureHandler(handlerTag, viewTag, actionType)` only stores the pair, so it is valid before the view exists.
- Events name their target by `view.id`: `RNGestureHandlerEvent.init(UIManagerHelper.getSurfaceId(view), view.id)` (react/RNGestureHandlerEvent.kt:34). `getSurfaceId` reads `ThemedReactContext.surfaceId` from `view.context` and otherwise returns -1 and logs a soft exception on every event.
- Three routes by `actionType` (react/RNGestureHandlerEventDispatcher.kt): **Reanimated worklet** (1) calls `ReanimatedModule.nodesManager.onEventDispatch(event)`; Reanimated routes by `(eventName, viewTag)` and never reads the surface id or a Fabric emitter (`NodesManager.onEventDispatch`, `nativeProxy/EventHandler.kt`); on the UI thread (the main thread) the worklet and Reanimated's mappers run in the same turn (`core.ts registerEventHandler` calls `__mapperRun` after every event). **JS function, new API** (4) is `reactApplicationContext.deviceEventEmitter.emit("onGestureHandlerEvent" | "onGestureHandlerStateChange", map)`; the payload carries `handlerTag`; no emitter. **Native animated event** (2) goes through `FabricUIManager.eventDispatcher.dispatchEvent`, which looks the tag up in React Native's own `SurfaceMountingManager` (`FabricUIManager.receiveEvent`: `mMountingManager.getEventEmitter(surfaceId, reactTag)`): engine tags are not there. That route is `Animated.event` with the native driver, which Svelte Native does not have, and is not supported.
- `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper(context: ReactContext, wrappedView: ViewGroup)` (react/RNGestureHandlerRootHelper.kt): needs `wrappedView.id >= 1` (:27), finds the first `com.facebook.react.uimanager.RootView` at or above `wrappedView` and throws without one (:30, :150), registers a root handler under tag `-wrappedView.id` (:43), and `tearDown` casts its context to `ThemedReactContext` (:54). When any other handler activates, the root handler is cancelled and calls `rootView.onChildStartedNativeGesture(rootView, cancelEvent)` (:98): that is how React Native cancels its JS responders. `requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent()` (a native view took the lock) cancels every handler. `dispatchTouchEvent(ev)` runs the orchestrator and returns `shouldIntercept`, which the wrapper turns into "swallow the rest of the gesture, children get nothing more".
- `NativeViewGestureHandler.onPrepare` lets a view implement `NativeViewGestureHandlerHook` (core/NativeViewGestureHandler.kt:84) instead of RNGH's class checks (`ReactScrollView`, `ReactViewGroup`, ...).
- `GestureHandler.prepare` finds the window offset through `view.context` (`ReactContext.currentActivity`, :168); engine views today use the Application context, which gives offset 0 and wrong `absoluteX/Y` in the `onTouches*` payloads (`GestureHandler.kt:418-465` subtract the offset).

iOS (`apple/`):
- `attachGestureHandler` looks the view up with `[RCTViewRegistry viewForReactTag:]` and retries 25 times at 0.1 s while the view is nil or has no superview, then gives up silently (`RNGestureHandlerManager.mm:44,143-170`). It sets `view.reactTag` and unwraps `RCTViewComponentView.contentView`.
- At the first attach it adds an `RNRootViewGestureRecognizer` to the first `RCTSurfaceView` ancestor (`registerViewWithGestureRecognizerAttachedIfNeeded`, :278; also `RCTFabricModalHostViewController` and `RNSFullWindowOverlayContainer`). That recogniser (a) cancels the surface's `RCTSurfaceTouchHandler` when an RNGH handler activates, which is how JS responders get their cancel, and (b) implements `enabled(false)`: `shouldBeRequiredToFailByGestureRecognizer` makes disabled handlers wait for it. No root component exists on iOS (`RNGestureHandlerRootViewComponentView.mm` returns nil).
- On the new architecture the module only queues operations; **`flushOperations` is what runs them** (`RNGestureHandlerModule.mm:212`). The detector must send it after every batch.
- Reanimated route: `[ReanimatedModule eventDispatcherWillDispatchEvent:]`; JS route: `sendDeviceEventWithName`. Both by `reactTag`.

JS and toolchain:
- `GestureStateManager` (`begin/activate/fail/end`) calls `Reanimated.setGestureState`, which is `global._setGestureState` on the UI runtime and only warns on the JS thread (`react-native-reanimated/src/platformFunctions/setGestureState.ts:14`). Reanimated's native side finds `RNGestureHandlerModule` by class name (`NativeProxy.kt`) and on iOS through `RNGestureHandlerStateManager.h`. So the state manager works in worklet callbacks only.
- `BaseGesture.shouldUseReanimated` is `runOnJS !== true && no callback is a non-worklet && !isRemoteDebuggingEnabled()`. A gesture with **no callbacks** therefore counts as a worklet gesture (action type 1): the golden vector `pan-no-callbacks-counts-as-worklet`. `isWorklet(cb)` is `cb.__workletHash !== undefined`.
- Measured with `transformer.ts` on a `.svelte` fixture: the worklets plugin auto-workletizes inline callbacks of `Gesture.<Name>().onX(cb)` chains whose receiver is literally the identifier `Gesture` (`react-native-worklets/plugin`, `gestureHandlerAutoworkletization`), after Svelte's compile. `onUpdate((e) => { x.value = e.translationX; })` came out with `__workletHash` and `__closure = { x }`. A callback that reads `$state` came out with `__closure = { $, count }` (the Svelte client namespace and the signal object, from `$.get(count)`), and one that reads a prop with `{ $$props, $, count }`. `import { Gesture as G }` or a callback held in a variable is not auto-workletized. `withSpring` is captured as the live binding of `@rasheed/svelte-native/animation`, which is a stub until Reanimated loads (see "Load order").
- The tag the host gives a node is `State.tag` (`fabric/host.ts`, `allocateTag`, even numbers from 2); the Fabric surface root tags are odd (1, 11, 21). Node tags are the keys of `MountingManager.registry` (Android) and of `RCTComponentViewRegistry` (iOS).

### Public API (Svelte)

```svelte
<script lang="ts">
  import { AnimatedView, createSharedValue, withSpring } from "@rasheed/svelte-native/animation";
  import { Gesture } from "@rasheed/svelte-native/gestures";

  const x = createSharedValue(0);   // first: it loads Reanimated, so the worklets below capture the real withSpring
  const y = createSharedValue(0);
  const pan = Gesture.Pan()
    .onUpdate((e) => { x.value = e.translationX; y.value = e.translationY; })   // UI thread: auto-workletized
    .onEnd(() => { x.value = withSpring(0); y.value = withSpring(0); });
</script>

<AnimatedView gesture={pan} style={{ width: 96, height: 96, borderRadius: 12, backgroundColor: "#3685ff" }}
  animatedStyle={() => { "worklet"; return { transform: [{ translateX: x.value }, { translateY: y.value }] }; }} />
```

Exports of `@rasheed/svelte-native/gestures` (new `exports` entry, `src/gestures/index.ts`):

| Export | Notes |
|---|---|
| `Gesture` | RNGH's `GestureObjects`, behind a lazy object: importing the entry loads nothing but five import-free constant modules; the first property read loads the pure builders **and Reanimated and worklets** (so worklets defined after it capture real functions). `Gesture.ForceTouch` is not exported (iOS only, deprecated upstream). |
| `gesture` attribute | `gesture?: GestureType \| ComposedGesture` on `View`, `Pressable`, `ScrollView`, `AnimatedView`, `Image`, `Text` and the `native-*` host tags (types in `elements.d.ts` and each component's props). Removing it, or setting it to `undefined`, detaches. |
| `<GestureDetector gesture {...viewProps}>` | `<native-view collapsable={false} {gesture}>`: **one extra view**, styled like a `View`. For porting React Native code and for children that do not forward attributes. |
| `use:gesture={g}` | Sugar for hand-written host tags and lowered primitives (`<View use:gesture={g}>`; `$.action` runs on the `NativeNode`, checked in compiled output). It sets the `gesture` attribute and removes it on destroy. `lower.ts` must stop warning that `use:` is "not run" for it. Not valid on a component. |
| `GestureStateManager`, `State`, `Directions`, `PointerType`, `TouchEventType`, `ActionType` constants, types (`GestureType`, `ComposedGesture`, `GestureUpdateEvent`, ...) | Re-exports (the manager lazily). |
| `runOnJS`, `scheduleOnRN` | Worklets' own, behind the same stub-then-real binding as `runOnUISync` in `animation.ts` (`animation.ts` gains them). |

Supported surface, all of it passed to RNGH's native handlers unchanged:
- Gestures: Tap, Pan, Pinch, Rotation, Fling, LongPress, Native, Manual, and the compositions Race, Simultaneous, Exclusive. Hover is passed through (the Android root forwards hover events) and is not in the acceptance matrix. ForceTouch: no.
- Config methods (RNGH 2.32 builders): `enabled`, `hitSlop` (number or object; negative values shrink the area on both platforms; a positive value grows it on Android only for a view whose parent does not clip, and not at all on iOS: RNGH's own limits), `shouldCancelWhenOutside`, `manualActivation`, `runOnJS`, `withRef` (a plain `{ current }` object), `withTestId`, `cancelsTouchesInView` (iOS), `needsPointerData` through `onTouchesDown/Move/Up/Cancelled`, `onBegin/onStart/onUpdate/onChange/onEnd/onFinalize`; Tap: `minPointers numberOfTaps maxDistance maxDuration maxDelay maxDeltaX maxDeltaY`; Pan: `activeOffsetX/Y failOffsetX/Y minPointers maxPointers minDistance minVelocity(X/Y) averageTouches activateAfterLongPress` (`enableTrackpadTwoFingerGesture` is iOS); LongPress: `minDuration maxDistance numberOfPointers`; Fling: `numberOfPointers direction`; Native: `shouldActivateOnStart disallowInterruption`. `activeCursor`, `mouseButton` (web), `userSelect`, `touchAction`, `enableContextMenu` are ignored.
- Relations: `simultaneousWithExternalGesture`, `requireExternalGestureToFail`, `blocksExternalGesture`, and the composition operators. Across components by gesture object; resolved by handler tag (JS contract).
- State manager: in `onTouches*` callbacks, **worklet callbacks only** (UI thread). On the JS thread it logs a development warning, as RNGH does.
- Thread rule (RNGH's): a gesture whose callbacks are all worklets (inline callbacks of a `Gesture.X().onY(cb)` chain are, automatically) and without `.runOnJS(true)` runs on the UI thread; any non-worklet callback, or `.runOnJS(true)`, puts the whole gesture on the JS thread; mixing is a development error (RNGH's own check, ported).
- Worklet closures capture SharedValues, numbers, strings, plain objects, other worklets and plain functions (remote, called through `runOnJS`). They **cannot read `$state`, `$derived` or `$props`**: the compile turns those into `$.get(...)`/`$$props.x`, and the closure then holds the Svelte namespace and the signal. Development builds check `__closure` of every UI callback (keys `$` or `$$props`, or a value shaped like a Svelte signal: fields `f`, `v`, `reactions`, `wv`) and throw naming the callback, the variable and the fix ("copy it into a SharedValue, or run the gesture on the JS thread with `.runOnJS(true)`"). JS-thread callbacks read live reactive state and need none of this.
- Load order: a worklet that captures `withSpring` from `@rasheed/svelte-native/animation` before Reanimated loads captures a stub, which the UI runtime rejects (the rule in `animation.ts`). `Gesture.Pan()` evaluates before the callbacks of its chain are created, so a chain is safe; a helper worklet defined above the first `Gesture` or `createSharedValue` call is not.
- Not supported, by design: the component API (`PanGestureHandler` and the other `*GestureHandler` components, `createNativeWrapper`), RNGH's `ScrollView`, `FlatList`, `Swipeable`, `ReanimatedSwipeable`, `DrawerLayout`, `RectButton` and the touchables, `GestureHandlerRootView` (not needed, not exported), `Animated.event` with the native driver, `useAnimatedGestureHandler`. A `Swipeable.svelte` built from the lab's row scenario is a later wave, not part of this work.
- A view that only carries a gesture is not an accessibility action; things that activate must be a `Pressable` (or declare accessibility actions). Tap gestures and `Pressable` both fire for the same tap, as in React Native (the JS responder hears the end before the gesture activates).

### JS contract

Files, all new unless marked, in `packages/svelte-native`:

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `src/fabric/gestures.ts` | The host side, loaded lazily like `animation.ts` (`loadGestures()`, on the first node that has a `gesture` attribute or once loaded; it loads Reanimated first, through the same `ensureReanimated()` as the animation layer): `gestureProps(node, target, props)`, `commitGestures(live)`, `sweepGestures(live)`, `detachGesture(node)`, `stats()` (records, native handlers, UI-thread registrations: the lab's leak check, also on `fabricStats().gestures`). Ends with `mark("gestures")`, so a `[Svelte boot]` line with that phase shows the host loaded. |
| `src/gestures/index.ts`, `src/components/GestureDetector.svelte`, `action.ts`, `types.ts` | The public entry and the sugar. `types.ts` holds `GestureAttribute`/`GestureProps` (what the primitives' prop types and `elements.d.ts` use) and re-exports RNGH's event types. `action.ts` takes the node by what it uses of it and must not import `renderer.ts`: type-checking an app that uses `use:gesture` would pull the renderer into its program, where `svelte/renderer` does not resolve (`apps/platform`'s `tsconfig.native.json`). |
| `src/gestures/rngh.{js,d.ts}`, `constants.{js,d.ts}` | The only files that name RNGH's source (the deep imports below); the `.d.ts` re-export RNGH's own declarations from `lib/typescript`, so the type checker never compiles its source. `constants` (State, Directions, PointerType, TouchEventType, ActionType: five import-free modules) is separate so the entry exports them without loading the builders. |
| `src/gestures/config.ts` | `ALLOWED_PROPS`, `filterConfig`, `extractGestureRelations`, `needsToReattach`: RNGH's, pure; `tests/gestures/config.test.ts` runs each against RNGH's own, bundled as an oracle. One deliberate difference: `extractGestureRelations` returns the same tag arrays but does not rewrite `config.requireToFail`/`simultaneousWith`/`blocksHandlers` in place (RNGH replaces the lists with the tags that exist and so forgets a gesture that has none yet; here they keep what the app named, duplicates removed, so a later mount can resolve them). |
| `src/gestures/guards.ts` | The development checks: `checkGestureCallbacksForWorklets` (RNGH's, some-and-some is an error, none at all a warning when the worklets plugin is on) and `checkUiClosures` (the Svelte-signal guard of "Public API"). Both are off when `__DEV__` is false. |
| `src/gestures/native.ts` | `nativeModule()` (`TurboModuleRegistry.getEnforcing("RNGestureHandlerModule")`, through `deps-rn.ts`), `defer` (RNGH's `ghQueueMicrotask`) and the one-time `enableNative()` = `SvelteEngine.enableGestures()` where the engine module exists (Android; `false` is an error, a throw propagates). |
| `src/gestures/events.ts` | The JS-thread receiver: a port of RNGH's `eventReceiver.ts` on `RCTDeviceEventEmitter` (`react-native/Libraries/EventEmitter/RCTDeviceEventEmitter`, already registered as a callable module by `init.ts`). Subscribes once, on the first attach. |
| `src/gestures/animated.ts` | The UI-thread path: the worklet of RNGH's `useAnimatedGesture`, ported (`createUiPath`, `rebuildUiPath`, `shareHandlers`, `disposeUiPath`), and `GestureStateManager.create`. `State`, `TouchEventType` and `CALLBACK_TYPE` are copied into it as local constants (a worklet captures by value; the file lists them by RNGH's names as `copiedConstants`, and the last test of `tests/gestures/config.test.ts` compares that list with RNGH's own modules, bundled apart, and fails on a numeric constant the list lacks). |
| `src/lazy-object.ts` | `lazyObject`, moved out of `animation.ts` (`Easing`, `ReduceMotion` and `Gesture` are lazy objects). |
| `src/fabric/host.ts` (edited) | The three call sites, next to the animation ones: in `build`, `const gestured = gestures || attributes.gesture !== undefined ? loadGestures().gestureProps(node, current, animated) : animated;` before `splitProps`; in `flush`, `gestures?.commitGestures(isLive)` after `animation?.commitAnimations()`; in `detachRemovedAnimations`, `gestures?.sweepGestures(isLive)`. Plus `ensureReanimated()` (loads Reanimated once and tells the engine once, for both layers), `isLive(node)` (still under a mounted surface) and `gesturesLoaded`/`gestures` on `fabricStats()`. |
| `src/fabric/deps-rn.ts` (edited) | `turboModuleRegistry()` and `deviceEventEmitter()`: lazy `require`s of `react-native/Libraries/TurboModule/TurboModuleRegistry` and `.../EventEmitter/RCTDeviceEventEmitter`, the only `react-native` imports of the gesture host. |
| `src/fabric/reanimated-internals.{js,d.ts}`, `src/animation.ts` (edited) | Export `WorkletEventHandler` from `react-native-reanimated/src/WorkletEventHandler`; export `runOnJS`, `scheduleOnRN`. |
| `src/lower.ts` (edited) | No `use:` warning for the `gesture` action imported from `@rasheed/svelte-native/gestures` (aliases included). |
| `tests/*.test.ts` (ten files), `tests/reanimated-stubs.ts` | The `react-native*` stub filter lets `react-native-gesture-handler/...` resolve to the installed package (RNGH's pure modules are in the host's bundle graph now, behind a lazy `require`); the Reanimated stubs gain `WorkletEventHandler`, a recording `makeMutable` and `setGestureState`. |

Reused from RNGH by deep import (`react-native-gesture-handler/src/...`, resolved by Metro like `reanimated-internals.js`): `handlers/gestures/{gestureObjects,gesture,gestureComposition,tapGesture,panGesture,pinchGesture,rotationGesture,flingGesture,longPressGesture,nativeGesture,manualGesture,hoverGesture,forceTouchGesture}.ts`, `handlers/{getNextHandlerTag,handlersRegistry}.ts`, `{mountRegistry,State,Directions,PointerType,TouchEventType,ActionType,utils}.ts`. **Never imported**: `index.ts`, `components/*`, `handlers/createHandler.tsx`, any `*GestureHandler.ts` component, `GestureDetector/*`, `eventReceiver.ts`, `reanimatedWrapper.ts`, `gestureStateManager.ts` at start (both eagerly `require` Reanimated), `findNodeHandle`. A test bundles the entry and fails on any of them.

**Lifecycle** (per node with a `gesture` attribute; one record per node, like `animation.ts`):
1. `gestureProps` (during `build`): returns the props without `gesture`, with `collapsable: false` forced (a flattened view has no native view and no tag; development warns once per node when the app passed `collapsable: true`). First time: makes the node's record. A changed `gesture` object marks the record for `update`; a removed (or `undefined`) attribute marks it for `detach`; a value that is not a gesture object throws.
2. `commitGestures(live)` (after `completeRoot`, so the shadow nodes exist; Android needs no view, iOS retries until the view mounts; `live` is the host's `isLive`): removals first, then new and changed records in tree order, each in its own try (the first failure is thrown after all were processed). A new record: claim its gesture objects (below), development checks, `enableNative()` once, `startListening()` once, `gesture.initialize()` (RNGH assigns handler tags), the UI-thread registration when any gesture runs there (before the native calls, so a failure leaves nothing native behind), then `createGestureHandler(handlerName, handlerTag, filterConfig(config, ALLOWED_PROPS))` and `registerHandler(tag, handler)` for every handler, then `attachGestureHandler(handlerTag, target.tag, actionType)` (`REANIMATED_WORKLET` = 1 when `shouldUseReanimated`, else `JS_FUNCTION_NEW_API` = 4) and `MountRegistry.gestureWillMount(gesture)` for every handler. Then **one** deferred batch (`setImmediate`, RNGH's `ghQueueMicrotask`; every record of the commit has its tags by then; a record is queued once however often it changed): for each record `gesture.prepare()` (a fresh attach), `updateGestureHandler(tag, filterConfig(config, ALLOWED_PROPS, extractGestureRelations(handler)))` (`waitFor`, `simultaneousHandlers`, `blocksHandlers` as tag arrays), then **one** `flushOperations()` for the whole batch, drops included. One difference from RNGH, which queues a flush per `scheduleFlushOperations` after each change: a commit that drops and attaches sends one flush after its updates.
3. Update (new gesture object): `needsToReattach` (different count, `handlerName`, or `shouldUseReanimated` per gesture) means drop and attach again with a rebuilt event handler (`updateEventHandler`; the registration is dropped when no gesture runs on the UI thread any more, made when one starts to); otherwise `updateHandlers`: `prepare()`, carry the old handler tags into the new objects (`newGestures[i].handlerTag = old.handlerTag`, `handlers.handlerTag` too), and in the batch copy `config` and `handlers` into the attached gestures, `updateGestureHandler` as above, re-register, and when a gesture's `gestureId` changed and any side runs on the UI thread, assign the shared callbacks again.
4. Relations across components: a global `MountRegistry.addMountListener` (RNGH's `useMountReactions`): when a gesture mounts that another record's `blocksHandlers`/`requireToFail`/`simultaneousWith` names (by object, by tag or through a `withRef` ref; RNGH's own check matches refs only), that record is updated (step 3 without reattach, queued once). Tags exist from `initialize()`; until then a relation to an unmounted gesture resolves to nothing (`tag > 0` filter), and sends again when it mounts because `extractGestureRelations` keeps what the app named. A record that is attaching does not react to its own gestures.
5. Detach (attribute removed, or `sweepGestures` after the node left the tree, which is how HMR remounts and screen pops end up here): per handler `dropGestureHandler(tag)`, `unregisterHandler(tag)`, `MountRegistry.gestureWillUnmount`, one `flushOperations()`, `WorkletEventHandler.unregisterFromEvents(tag)`, the shared callbacks set to null. `stopSurface` calls the sweep, so a hot remount drops every handler before the next surface creates its own.
6. UI-thread path (`animated.ts`), per record that has any `shouldUseReanimated` gesture: `sharedHandlers = makeMutable(null)`, `lastUpdateEvent = makeMutable([])`, `callback` = the worklet of `useAnimatedGesture` (state-change events to `onBegin/onStart/onEnd/onFinalize`, touch events with a `GestureStateManager.create(handlerTag)`, update events to `onUpdate` and `onChange` through `changeEventCalculator`; the dispatch rules are the ones of `eventReceiver.ts`), `handler = new WorkletEventHandler(callback, ["onGestureHandlerStateChange", "onGestureHandlerEvent"])`, `handler.registerForEvents(target.tag)`, `sharedHandlers.value = attached.filter(shouldUseReanimated).map(g => g.handlers)` inside a try; when Reanimated cannot serialise the value, each callback is sent alone to find the one that fails and the error names it and its gesture. On reattach, `handler.updateEventHandler(callback, names)`. This is `useEvent` without the hook (`hook/useEvent.ts` is `new WorkletEventHandler` plus a ref).
7. JS-thread path: one `RCTDeviceEventEmitter` subscription pair, started on the first attach, routes by `handlerTag` through `handlersRegistry.findHandler` with RNGH's dispatch rules and a state manager stub (development warning). A gesture that is on the UI thread is never delivered here.
8. Gestures must not be shared between nodes: one `Gesture` object has one `handlerTag`. At commit every gesture object is claimed by its record (a `WeakMap`); a second record that claims one throws RNGH's native message (`Handler with tag N already exists. Please ensure that no Gesture instance is used across multiple GestureDetectors.`) before any native call for it, in development and in release alike. An owner whose node left the tree but was not swept yet (a gesture object handed to the next node of a keyed block) is detached first, so that is not an error.
9. Development checks (`__DEV__` only; the SPEC's risk item about release is decided: **off in release**): before a gesture's handlers are created or updated, `checkGestureCallbacksForWorklets` and `checkUiClosures` throw or log as described in "Public API".

**Host additions in `fabric/host.ts`** are the three call sites above plus the helpers in the table. The tag is `target.tag`.

**HMR**: a hot update remounts the surface (`fabric/hmr.ts`), which unmounts, sweeps and recreates; handler tags are fresh (RNGH's counter lives in a module that is not on the path to an edited file and survives). The test remounts twice and checks the call log.

### Android contract

The engine owns the View tree, so everything RNGH reads from a `View` must be true of engine views. `SvelteEngine.enableGestures()` (new `Function`, called by `gestures/native.ts` before the first `attachGestureHandler`, runs the install on the main thread with `onMainBlocking`) installs it lazily so an app that never uses gestures loads no RNGH class; the engine references RNGH with `compileOnly project(':react-native-gesture-handler')` and guards the install with `NoClassDefFoundError`.

1. **Ids.** Every engine view has `View.id == node tag` (`MountingManager.createView`, after `registry.put`; reset to `NO_ID` when pooled), and `isSaveEnabled = false` (an id makes Android save and restore the view's state across recreation: `EngineInput` text, `EngineScroll` offset). `EngineRoot.id` is the constant `0x7E000000` (positive for RNGH's assertion, above any tag, below the aapt range). `EngineForeign` frames keep `NO_ID`: the foreign view inside carries the tag (React Native sets it). `EngineHeader`'s `View.generateViewId()` ids (EngineHeader.kt:292) move to a private counter from `0x70000000`: `generateViewId` counts up from 1, so its ids equal the low tags (2, 4, 6, ...) of the nodes created at start, which live as long as the app.
2. **Context.** Engine views are created with one `ThemedReactContext(reactContext, reactContext, "SvelteEngine", surfaceId)` built at the first batch (where `root.surfaceId` is assigned, `MountingManager.apply`), not the bare Application context. Reasons: `UIManagerHelper.getSurfaceId(view)` needs it (no soft exception per event), and `GestureHandler.prepare` finds the Activity through `ReactContext.currentActivity` (correct `absoluteX/Y` in touch payloads). The surface id inside is read by nobody that matters (Reanimated ignores it), so a recreated Activity's new surface id does not need new views. The same object is the context of the root helper.
3. **What the orchestrator reads.** `EngineNode` extends `ReactPointerEventsView` with `pointerEvents` mapped from `node.pointer` (0 auto, 1 none, 2 box-none, 3 box-only), and `EngineScreen` with `touchable == false` answers NONE: every rule `HitTest` applies beyond `visibility`, alpha and bounds must be expressed this way, because RNGH does not call `HitTest`. `ModalLayer` gets a transparent `ColorDrawable` background: RNGH treats a `ViewGroup` without a background as see-through (`shouldHandlerlessViewBecomeTouchTarget`), so a gesture could otherwise start under a modal's empty area. Clipping already matches (`clipChildren == overflow hidden`, `ScrollView` clips).
4. **Root.** `EngineRoot : FrameLayout, RootView`. `onChildStartedNativeGesture(child, ev) = cancelActiveTouch()` (JavaScript gets its one cancel and nothing more until the next DOWN: that is the React Native contract for "a native gesture started"), `onChildEndedNativeGesture` does nothing, `handleException` forwards to `reactContext.handleException`. `enableGestures` creates `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper(themedContext, this)` (this is the one place that needs the Android `ReactContext.getNativeModule(RNGestureHandlerModule)`: the package must be in the autolinked list). `OnDestroy` calls `tearDown()`.
5. **Order inside `EngineRoot.dispatchTouchEvent`** (the exact sequence; steps 1 and 2 exist today):
   1. DOWN hit test (`onEngine`), as now. A gesture that starts on no engine view is not offered to RNGH either.
   2. JavaScript's touch dispatch, as now (React Native's root view also forwards to JS in `onInterceptTouchEvent`, before its children and before `RNGestureHandlerRootView` sees the event, `ReactRootView.java:261`, `ReactSurfaceView.kt:133`, so a `Pressable` and a Tap gesture both fire for one tap, in both stacks).
   3. **The claim** (lane G6), on a single pointer MOVE of a gesture no handler owns yet and no native view has taken the lock of: the ancestors of the DOWN's target that implement `NativeClaim` (`EngineStack` in its edge zone, `EngineSheet`; collected at the DOWN, outermost first) are asked whether the finger's travel from the DOWN is theirs (`claims(dx, dy)`: a touch slop away, mostly along their axis, `GestureRules.edgeSwipe`/`sheetDrag`; a sheet also needs its scroll view to be unable to scroll that way). Without this step Android asks them only in `super.dispatchTouchEvent`, after the helper, and the helper activates a default Pan on the very event that crosses the slop. The MOVE's history samples (`getHistoricalX`) are walked oldest first and the first sample that crosses decides: the handlers are first given a MOVE at the sample before it (a probe), so a pan that activates earlier (`activeOffsetY([-4, 4])` against a sheet's 8 dp) keeps the gesture; when none does, the view `claim`s it (`startDrag`: JavaScript's cancel and the touch lock, `requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(true)`, which cancels every handler through the helper's disallow path) and the event goes on. The stack's and the sheet's `onInterceptTouchEvent` answer true while their drag runs so that Android cancels the child. Tie rule, in one place: **on one sample, the native view wins; a handler wins only by activating on an earlier sample.** Nothing of this runs until gestures are enabled.
   4. `intercept = gestureRoot.dispatchTouchEvent(ev)` (inside `Trace.beginSection("SvelteEngine.gesture")`), for every event of an engine gesture, pointer events included. When a handler activates inside this call, RNGH cancels its root handler, which calls `onChildStartedNativeGesture` and JavaScript's responders get their cancel.
   5. If `intercept`, return true **without** `super.dispatchTouchEvent(ev)`: children get nothing more of the gesture (parity with `RNGestureHandlerRootView`; the next DOWN's `ViewGroup` reset cancels the stale touch target). Otherwise `super.dispatchTouchEvent(ev)` as now, then return true.
   - `dispatchGenericMotionEvent(ev)`: hover actions go to `gestureRoot.dispatchTouchEvent(ev)` (as `RNGestureHandlerRootView` does). `requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(disallow)`: `gestureRoot.requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent()` first, then super.
6. **Native views take the lock through Android's protocol.** `ScrollView` and `HorizontalScrollView` call `parent.requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(true)` when they start dragging, which now cancels every RNGH handler; `EngineSheet.startDrag` already does (EngineSheet.kt:346). **`EngineStack.startDrag` (the edge swipe) does not and must**, with `false` at the end like the sheet, or an RNGH pan under the swipe keeps its handler alive next to it.
7. **`Gesture.Native()` on a scroll view.** `EngineScroll` and `EngineHScroll` implement `NativeViewGestureHandler.NativeViewGestureHandlerHook`: `shouldCancelRootViewGestureHandlerIfNecessary() = true` (RNGH's `ScrollViewHook`) and `sendTouchEvent(view, ev)` = `bridge.onDispatch(ev)` then `onTouchEvent(ev)`, because RNGH calls `onTouchEvent` directly once the handler feeds the view and the bridge's velocity tracker lives in `dispatchTouchEvent`. Other native nodes (`EngineInput`, `EngineSwitch`) are not wrapped in v1.
8. **Events to JavaScript need no engine work.** The Reanimated and device-event routes never touch `SurfaceMountingManager` or a Fabric emitter (facts above); the engine's own `dispatchTouch` path is not involved. `SvelteEngine.stats()` gains nothing.
9. **Packaging.** The autolinked `RNGestureHandlerPackage` (TurboModule created lazily; `libgesturehandler.so` is only loaded by `install()`, which nothing calls) goes through the on-demand wrapper so its two view managers are not created at start: `androidOnDemandViewManagers["com.swmansion.gesturehandler.RNGestureHandlerPackage"] = ["RNGestureHandlerRootView", "RNGestureHandlerButton"]` in `app.config.ts`, and `tests/foreign.test.ts`'s name check extended.
10. **Threads.** The module's `@ReactMethod`s run on the native-modules thread and RNGH's registry is `@Synchronized`; the orchestrator runs on the main thread. Reanimated's UI runtime is the main thread, so worklet callbacks run inside step 5.4, on the same frame as the touch.

**Android status (lane G2, 28 Oct 2026).** Items 1 to 10 are implemented in `android/src/main/java/expo/modules/svelteengine/`: `GestureRules.kt` (the id constants and the pointer-events mapping, no Android type), `EngineGestures.kt` (the only file that names `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper`), `views/EngineRoot.kt` (`RootView`, `GestureRoot`, the touch order), `MountingManager.kt` (`ThemedReactContext`, ids, `enableGestures`/`disableGestures`), `views/{EngineView,EngineScreen,EngineScroll,EngineStack,EngineModal,EngineHeader,ViewPool}.kt`, `SvelteEngineModule.kt` (`enableGestures`), `build.gradle`, and `app.config.ts` with the extended name check in `tests/foreign.test.ts`. Where the build differs from the text above:
- `enableGestures` waits 5 s for the main thread (not `onMainBlocking`'s 500 ms), answers `false` only when the library is not in the app, and throws when the main thread does not answer or RNGH's module is not registered. It is idempotent.
- `requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(false)` does not reach the root helper: only the request to take the lock cancels handlers (RNGH's own root view forwards both; the difference is a cancel at the end of a gesture that has nothing left to cancel).
- `EngineScroll` and `EngineHScroll` name one RNGH interface (`NativeViewGestureHandlerHook`), so that interface class loads with the first scroll view; `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper`, the orchestrator and every handler load at the first `enableGestures`. RNGH is a hard dependency of the app, so this is a class load, not a failure mode.
- RNGH walks `getChildAt(i)` in index order, not the engine's z order (`EngineView.hitChildIndex`): two overlapping siblings with different `zIndex` or `elevation` are offered to a gesture in the order of insertion (React Native's own `ReactViewGroup` has the same limit).
- `HitTest` skips nothing on alpha; RNGH skips a view below alpha 0.1. A view faded below 0.1 receives JavaScript's touches and no gesture.
- Lab switch: the launch intent extra `svelteGestures` (boolean), or `?gestures=1` on the launch link, makes the engine call `enableGestures()` at install, as the first gesture would; `svelteEngine false` runs the build on React Native's own mounting layer. They change what the engine does, so only the QA app (`io.rasheed.svelteqa`) and a debuggable build read them (`LabSwitches.kt`, host-checked: a release build does not even look at the intent). The harness flag is `--gestures` on `lab`, `parity`, `a11y` and `resilience` (`launchWithGestures` in `qa/lib/adb.ts`: every `am start` of the run carries the extra). The link form reaches only a launch that has a link; the extra reaches parity's launcher-activity cases too.
- Checks that ran: `:svelte-engine:compileReleaseKotlin` and `:app:assembleRelease` (x86_64, with RNGH 2.32.0 built from source against React Native 0.86.3: CMake with `-Werror` and the Kotlin sources compile; the generated `MainApplication` wraps `RNGestureHandlerPackage` on demand), and the host check `android/src/test/run.sh` (kotlinc and `java`: 2039 checks of the pointer-events mapping against React Native's own `PointerEvents` predicates and against the numbers `HitTest` reads, and of the id rules). Device results are in the next paragraph.

**Android device results (OVH emulator, `localhost:5555`, Android 16 x86_64 under software GL, up three days at the time: frame numbers are informational; 31 Oct and 1 Nov 2026).** Source commit `bb2365f4c` plus the SPEC.
- Engine unchanged with gestures never enabled and with the root helper installed at every launch (`--gestures`; the launch logs carry `[GESTURE HANDLER] Initialize gesture handler for root view` and `SvelteEngine: gestures enabled` in that mode only): `lab --run checks,quick --repeat 3` complete, 28/28 checks, 0 fatals, 0 engine warnings, in both modes (143 s a run); `parity` 200 pass, 0 fail, 0 px, 0 unstable, in both modes (10 routes not covered, as before). Every frame verdict of the lab that failed on this emulator failed before.
- `native-qa gestures --input adb --run box,box-js,row,carousel,scroll-native,churn`, three repeats: `box-js` (Pan with `runOnJS(true)`, callbacks on the JavaScript thread) begin 1, start 1, update 23, end(success) 1, finalize 1, back at 0; `box` (the same Pan with worklet callbacks on the UI runtime, a spring on release) begin 1, start 1, update 22, end(success) 1, finalize 1, back at 0; `row`, `carousel`, `scroll-native` and `churn` (records, native handlers and UI registrations 0, engine views back to the baseline of 130) pass 7/7, 7/7, 5/5 and 6/6 in every repeat. `box` is judged NOT SMOOTH (3 to 6 % dropped frames against the 1 % bar), like the lab's own animated scenarios on this emulator; one `box` and one `box-js` launch read a peak of 143 of 160 dp (`input swipe` stops short of its end; the in-app injector delivers the exact path).
- The rest, with `--input both` once: `longpress-tap` 11/11 with both drivers; `pushed-edge`, `pinch-rotate`, `sheet` and `carousel` failed in ways lane G6 traced and fixed (the next paragraph).
- Two defects the lab found, both fixed: a function-valued `ref` in a spread (`{...probe(name)}`) was dropped (Svelte's `set_attributes` skips a function value that is not an `on*` handler, and an element with a spread sends all of its attributes through it; lane G6 fixed it in `lower.ts` and the renderer, see below), and a pooled `EngineScroll` kept its offset when its content was deleted first (`ScrollView.scrollTo` does nothing without a child), which showed on the next owner and put the carousel's measured rectangle 621 px from where the swipe landed.

**Android arbitration (lane G6, 1 Nov 2026; OVH emulator, fresh instance; source `050b2fda4` plus the SPEC).** Each cause below was read from the `SvelteGesture` trace (`adb shell setprop log.tag.SvelteGesture VERBOSE`, then `adb logcat -s SvelteGesture`: every DOWN, every move with its history size and whether a handler owns the gesture afterwards, every claim and probe, the sheet's release; off by default, one boolean read per event).
- **A function-valued `ref` in a spread.** `lower.ts` wraps the spreads of a lowered tag, and the attributes beside them, in the renderer's `hostSpread`/`hostValue` (a function becomes a cached box, imported on the script's opening line so no line moves) and `setAttribute` unboxes it, so `ref` and any non-event function attribute reach the host as they do without a spread. Compiled-fixture test `tests/gestures/spread.test.ts` (a ref in a spread, beside one, after one; a `ScrollView` and a `Text`); the lab pages are spreads again.
- **Sheet.** (b) The grabber step: `sheet release: y=239.9 dp, offsets=0, 433 dp, velocity=-343 dp/s, target=432.8 dp`. The sheet settles at the detent nearest to where the finger lets go, its drag starts after the slop (the first 19.8 dp of the finger are not the sheet's), and the detents are 433 dp apart, so the 220 dp the lab dragged went back. The lab drags 330 dp (past half the travel plus the slop, under a fling in 400 ms). (a) The ScrollView's aim at y = 3082 followed from (b): the sheet had not risen, and a view taller than the visible part of the sheet has most of its rectangle under the screen; the page now reports the part of each rectangle that is on the screen. (c) The default Pan: `ACTION_MOVE t=24 ms ... -> gesture handlers: a handler owns the gesture` with the sheet's `onInterceptTouchEvent` never called for that event: the pan activated on the move that crossed 8 dp, and the helper runs before the children. The SPEC's premise was wrong: `PanGestureHandler.defaultMinDist` is `getScaledTouchSlop`, the sheet's slop, not 10 dp, so the two always cross on the same move and the order inside `dispatchTouchEvent` decided. Fixed in the engine by the claim (contract item 5.3), not in the lab.
- **Edge swipe with the injector.** `ACTION_DOWN x=8.0 dp`, then `ACTION_MOVE t=17 ms x=20.2 dp -> a handler owns the gesture`: one frame carried the finger 12.2 dp, past the stack's 8 dp and the pan's `activeOffsetX` 10 dp, and the helper ran first. After the claim: `sample 0 of 2 before the claim -> none active`, `EngineStack claims the gesture at sample 1 of 2 (dx=9.2 dp)`, and the pan never starts. (`adb input` passed because its first move happened to land between 8 and 10 dp.) A vertical drag from the edge is not the swipe (`dx > |dy|` is part of the rule, host-checked) and reaches a vertical pan.
- **Carousel.** Not the engine's scroll interception: in the failing `adb` run the move after `(-7.6, -4.4) dp` was `(-20.9, -12.1) dp`, past `activeOffsetX` 10 and `failOffsetY` 10 together, `PanGestureHandler.onHandle` checks `shouldFail` before `shouldActivate`, the pan failed (`no handler active` for the rest of the gesture) and the list, whose slop is 8 dp, took the touch. At 30 degrees an event has to land with 10 to 17.3 dp across; a 300 ms flick moves 10.6 dp per frame, so a frame that comes late (the emulator drops 9 to 20 % of them) misses the window. The injector now catches a late frame up with the moves it missed (one per period) and each move holds history samples, so what is judged is the arbitration and not the emulator's frame jitter; the lab's diagonal takes 600 ms (5.3 dp per frame), which no single late frame can jump over.
- **Pinch and rotation.** The engine loses nothing; the harness's expectation was wrong. RNGH counts from where each handler activates: `ScaleGestureDetector` begins after the span moved two slops, `PinchGestureHandler` activates one slop later and `activate()` resets `scale` to 1; `RotationGestureHandler` activates at 5 degrees and resets `rotation` to 0. A 3x spread through 40 degrees therefore reads 2.21 and 34 degrees, which `expectedTwoFinger` now replays from the injector's event times (the lab checks within 5 % and 3 degrees; measured 2.211 and 34.0 in every run). iOS does the same (`RNPinchHandler.m` sets `scale = 1` and `RNBetterRotationRecognizer.handleGesture` sets `rotation = 0` at `Began`; the iPhone's XCUITest read 32.5 for a commanded 40), so without the injector's report the lab expects 34 +- 6 degrees, not 40 +- 5, on both platforms.
- **`box` with `input swipe`.** A complete drag reads 138 to 148 of 160 dp there (a Pan's translation starts at its activation, 7 dp in, and `input swipe` sends its last MOVE before the end of its path), so the bar for the peak is 80 % with `adb` and 90 % with the injector.
- **RNGH next to plain React Native (nothing changed).** (1) The orchestrator walks `viewGroup.getChildAt(i)` from the last child (`GestureHandlerOrchestrator.kt:601`), not `getChildDrawingOrder`; `HitTest` uses `EngineView.hitChildIndex`, which sorts by elevation and then zIndex.
