Fabric orders the children of a stacking context by zIndex before the mount, so the two agree for zIndex (on the device two overlapping `Pressable`s with a Tap each, the one with zIndex 2 declared first: JavaScript's press and RNGH's tap both went to it); elevation is the only order RNGH does not see, and React Native's `ReactViewGroup` has the same limit. (2) A view with alpha below 0.1 is skipped with its subtree (`RNGestureHandlerRootHelper.MIN_ALPHA_FOR_TOUCH` into `minimumAlphaForTraversal`; `HitTest` looks at visibility only): on the device a view at opacity 0.05 with a Tap received none and its `Pressable` still pressed. (3) Clipping is visible to RNGH: `Props.kt` sets `clipChildren = (overflow == hidden)` on every engine view and `RNViewConfigurationHelper.isViewClippingChildren` reads it, so a touch outside an `overflow: hidden` parent never reaches its children, and with `overflow: visible` it does (device: a Tap on a child that sticks out of a hidden parent did not fire, the same on a visible parent did, and both fired inside the bounds). No engine change was needed for any of the three.
- **Results** (`gestures --input both --repeat 3`, all scenarios; frame verdicts `NOT SMOOTH` on `box`, `box-busy` and `pinch-rotate` are the emulator's software GL and are informational): every scenario passes every check with both drivers in all three launches of each (`box`, `box-busy`, `box-js`, `box-js-busy`, `row` 7/7, `carousel` 7/7, `pushed-edge` 5/5, `sheet` 6/6, `longpress-tap` 11/11, `scroll-native` 5/5, `churn` 6/6; `pinch-rotate` 6/6 with the injector, which is its only driver), 0 fatals, 0 engine warnings, 0 harness errors; the one exception, `box-busy` with `adb` missing its 90 % peak bar by 3 dp in three of three launches, is the `input swipe` tail above and now has its own bar. `lab --run checks,quick --repeat 3`: complete, 28/28, 0 fatals, 0 engine warnings, with and without `--gestures` (143 s a run); `parity --gestures`: 199 of 200 at 0 px on the first pass; the one miss, `tree-inbox--dark-ltr-normal`, failed its "anchor is on screen" check (the inbox had not drawn its content from the backend, 946037 px) and passes at 0 px in the re-run of the eight `tree-inbox` cases with `--gestures`; 10 routes not covered, as before. After the last change to the box's bar (checks only), `box`, `box-busy`, `box-js` and `box-js-busy` with `adb` pass 7/7 in three launches; the tunnel to the emulator dropped before the `pinch-rotate` launch of that last build could run, so its 6/6 (three launches, above) is from the build one commit before, whose only difference is the iOS-side rotation expectation (no injector report).
- **Review fixes (1 Nov 2026), Android.** (1) *A claim never follows an activated handler.* A handler can activate between two events (a LongPress timer, `Pan.activateAfterLongPress`); RNGH then cancels its root handler, which calls `onChildStartedNativeGesture`, and `handlerOwns` stayed false: the next move was claimed, the touch lock cancelled nothing (the root handler is no longer BEGAN), and `dispatchTouchEvent` returned true before `super`, so the stack or sheet stayed `dragging` with the lock held. `EngineRoot.onChildStartedNativeGesture` now marks the gesture as the handlers'. (2) *The claim measures on the DOWN pointer.* It and the stack's and sheet's own intercept compared pointer index 0 with the DOWN position: finger A down in the edge zone, B at the centre, A up, B moves popped the stack. The DOWN pointer is recorded, forgotten when it lifts (Android hands ids out again) and found with `findPointerIndex`; the claim is skipped when it is gone or a second finger is down. (3) The decision is a pure `ClaimArbiter` (`ClaimRules.kt`: history walk, probe, `handlerOwns` and lock gates), covered in the host check with async activation, multi-pointer, id reuse and the outermost-claimer order; the distance rule it is tied to is read from RNGH's `PanGestureHandler.shouldActivate` source in the check, not retyped. (4) `Pan.minDistance(n)` with `n` larger than the touch slop never beats the edge swipe or a sheet's drag: they claim at the slop (`GestureRules.edgeSwipe`, `sheetDrag`), the pan activates at `n`, so the claim comes first and the pan is cancelled. Use `activeOffsetX`/`activeOffsetY` (smaller than the slop) to win a tie. After the fixes, on the OVH emulator (QA APK built from them, 1 Nov): `box`, `pushed-edge`, `sheet` and `longpress-tap` pass every check with `adb input` (4 of 4 scenarios) and with the in-app injector, which is now the `svelte-qa-android` module (`box`, `pinch-rotate`, `pushed-edge`, `sheet`, `longpress-tap`: 5 of 5 scenarios, frame verdicts informational as above). The asynchronous activation and the two-finger case have no lab scenario: they are held by the host check only. A first adb run of the same day failed every scenario because SystemUI had an "Application Not Responding" dialog in front of the app (it took the focus; "Wait" cleared it) and was repeated.

### iOS contract

iOS mounts through React Native, so the view lookup works as in any Fabric app: `SvelteStackView` and `SvelteScreenView` are `RCTViewComponentView`s the registry knows by tag, and pushed screens hang below the stack view, which hangs below the `RCTSurfaceView`. What has to be done and checked:
1. Nothing in JS beyond the shared contract: the module is autolinked (pods), `enableGestures` is Android only, no root view is rendered. `flushOperations` after every batch is mandatory (golden vectors include it). The detector attaches after the commit, inside RNGH's 2.5 s retry window; a node that mounts later than that (a hidden screen) must attach when it mounts: **verify** with a gesture on a tab pane that was not selected at launch.
2. **Presented content is outside the surface.** Sheets, modals and header slots put their content under `SvelteTouchHost` (`SvelteSupport.mm:125`), an `RCTSurfaceTouchHandler` attached to the presented view, which is not below an `RCTSurfaceView`. RNGH then installs no root recogniser there: an activated gesture would not cancel `SvelteTouchHost`'s JS touches, and `enabled(false)` would not hold. Fix: `patches/react-native-gesture-handler@2.32.0.patch` (entry in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `patchedDependencies`) changes the loop of `registerViewWithGestureRecognizerAttachedIfNeeded` to stop at the first view that has an `RCTSurfaceTouchHandler` among its recognisers (about six lines; generalises the `RCTFabricModalHostViewController` special case). Without a patch: `SvelteTouchHost attachToView:` adds `NSClassFromString(@"RNRootViewGestureRecognizer")` itself with a delegate whose `gestureRecognizer:didActivateInViewWithTouchHandler:` toggles `RCTSurfaceTouchHandler.enabled` (NO, YES). React Native's iOS is a prebuilt XCFramework, but RNGH is a pod built from source, so the patch takes effect.
3. **Pop gesture.** `interactivePopGestureRecognizer`'s delegate is `SvelteStackView` (`SvelteStackView.mm`), and RNGH's handlers decline to coordinate with recognisers that are not RNGH's (`RNGestureHandler.mm` returns NO from `shouldRequireFailureOf`/`shouldBeRequiredToFailBy` for them). Policy: **the edge swipe wins in its zone, content pans win elsewhere, and a pan waits for the swipe only where the swipe can still begin.** `SvelteStackView gestureRecognizer:shouldBeRequiredToFailByGestureRecognizer:` answers YES for an RNGH pan while the swipe can begin (`gestureRecognizerShouldBegin:`). **The zone is UIKit's:** the recogniser is a `UIScreenEdgePanGestureRecognizer` subclass (`_UIParallaxTransitionPanGestureRecognizer`, edges left) whose region this code cannot change, and it is the same with UIKit's own delegate. On the iPhone SE 3 (iOS 26.6) it takes the synthesized touches of XCUITest that start 1 to 3 pt from the edge, fails 4 to 12 pt at touch-down and never sees a touch from 16 pt; a finger's contact is larger than a synthesized point, so a person's zone differs from this figure. The lab aims 2 pt on iOS (`native-qa gestures --platform ios`, `--edge-x` overrides; Android keeps 8 dp). The iOS 26 full-screen pop (`interactiveContentPopGestureRecognizer`) stays disabled (`SvelteStackView.mm`); measured as the input for a later decision: enabled, its strip is about 12 pt (x = 4 to 12 began, 16 and beyond never saw the touch) and over a full-width Pan the Pan still won from 8 pt, so it would not give the lab's 8 pt either. Verified with touches, with the numbers, under "iOS status (lane G5)". The app-level escape is `hitSlop({ left: -24 })` on the pan.
4. **Sheets.** UIKit's detent drag is a `UIPanGestureRecognizer` (delegate `_UISheetInteraction`) on the `UIDropShadowView` that holds the presented view, an ancestor of the screen view; it is private and nothing coordinates it. **Verified with touches (lane G5): UIKit's own exclusivity gives the Android policy, the first recogniser to activate wins and the loser fails at that moment and never moves**, so no code was written for it: the grabber moves the sheet, a ScrollView at the top detent scrolls, a default Pan and a Pan with `activeOffsetY([-4, 4])` win and the sheet stays (numbers under "iOS status (lane G5)"). A default Pan's tie with the sheet's drag went to the Pan, where Android's sheet wins it.
5. **Scroll views** are React Native's own `RCTScrollViewComponentView`: `Gesture.Native()` and relations with them are RNGH's tested path (`RNGestureHandler.mm areScrollViewRecognizersCompatible`).
6. `SvelteQA` gains a probe `gestureRecognizers(tag)` (class, state, delegate class of every recogniser on the view with that tag, and of its surface view) so the harness can assert "the root recogniser exists" and "the handler is attached" without touch synthesis.

#### iOS status (lane G3, 28 Oct 2026)

Implemented and run on the iPhone SE 3 (iOS 26.6, 60 Hz), branch head `67f4fcf82`, probes `gestures-surface`, `gestures-sheet`, `gestures-pop` (`lib/native/dev/probe.ts`, page `/__dev/gesture-probe`, `SvelteQA.gestureRecognizers(tag)`; runs in `qa/runs/probe-E74A5608-...-20261028T101245Z-ltr`). The probe attaches handlers through RNGH's native module (`createGestureHandler`, `attachGestureHandler`, `flushOperations`) and reads UIKit's recognisers; it does not touch the screen. Before the device: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts` (the patch applies on the Mac too), `pod install`, `clang -fsyntax-only` and `swiftc -typecheck` of the edited files.

- **Root recogniser outside the surface (item 2).** RNGH registers its root recogniser *when a handler attaches*, by walking up from the handler's view to a view that holds React Native's touch handler. A screen's view has no superview at that moment (UIKit places it after the commit that mounted it: staged a frame later, presented later still), so the walk ends at nil and RNGH installs nothing, for a sheet and for a pushed screen alike. Two parts: (a) `patches/react-native-gesture-handler@2.32.0.patch` (`patchedDependencies`, hash in the lockfile; one helper and one condition in the loop of `registerViewWithGestureRecognizerAttachedIfNeeded`) stops the walk at the first view that holds an `RCTSurfaceTouchHandler`; (b) `SvelteEnsureGestureRoot(view)` (`SvelteSupport.mm`; `NSClassFromString`, no pod dependency on RNGH) asks RNGH's manager to do at the screen's own time what it could not do at attach time. It runs when a `SvelteScreenView` joins its window and when `SvelteTouchHost attachToView:` runs, and is a nil check in an app with no handler. The patch (a) also adds two methods to `RNGestureHandlerManager`: `+currentManager` (the manager of the running module, nil before the module exists) and `-registerRootViewGestureRecognizerIfAttachedBelow:`, which loops over the manager's own handlers (not the views of the subtree) and, when one is attached below `view`, runs the library's own `registerViewWithGestureRecognizerAttachedIfNeeded:`. The recogniser is therefore in `_rootViewGestureRecognizers` (`handleSetJSResponder:` reaches it) and the manager is its delegate (its activation callback cancels JavaScript's touches). Until 1 Nov the module made the recogniser itself, with a delegate of its own, outside that set, and walked the whole subtree of a screen on every `didMoveToWindow` and `attachToView:` until one existed, which in an app with no gesture is forever; the lockfile's `patch_hash` is the sha256 of the patch (a test holds it). **Not compiled or run: needs the Mac.** What follows was measured on the earlier version of (b) and has to be measured again. **Verified (recognisers, not touches):** in a fresh process the first handler of the process, attached as a pushed page mounted, left a root recogniser on the `RCTSurfaceView` (next to its `RCTSurfaceTouchHandler`) whose delegate is `SvelteGestureRootDelegate`, i.e. RNGH's own registration had not run: (b) is needed. A sheet whose handler attached as the page mounted has the root recogniser on its `SvelteScreenView` (the host `SvelteTouchHost` listens on; there is no root recogniser on the surface for it), delegate `SvelteGestureRootDelegate`. A sheet presented with no handler had none; after a handler attached to it the root recogniser was there with delegate `RNGestureHandlerManager`: RNGH's own registration, which unpatched climbs past the sheet's views to the window (source), so that is (a). Lane G5: that an activated handler cancels JavaScript's touches is verified (`gestures-longpress-tap`: the press is cancelled when the long press activates); **not verified:** that `enabled(false)` holds through the root (the recogniser of a disabled handler does report `enabled: false`).
- **A handler attached after mount is bound (item 1).** Verified: handlers attached as the page mounted and ones attached after the screen was in place (a view in a scroll view, a sheet's view, a view of the root screen) each left an `RNBetterPanGestureRecognizer` on the view (delegate `RNPanGestureHandler`, `handlerTag` the number given), `enabled: false` after `updateGestureHandler({ enabled: false })`, and none after `dropGestureHandler`. A gesture on a tab pane that was not selected (lane G5, 31 Oct, probe `gestures-tab-pane`, page `/__dev/gesture-probe?mode=tabs|tabs-eager`, build with main.jsbundle `d616abee975b`): **bound, verified by the recogniser, not by a touch.** With `lazy={false}` (the pane is mounted from the start and hidden), a handler attached as the pane mounted had its `RNBetterPanGestureRecognizer` on the view 4 s later, after RNGH's 2.5 s of attach retries was over, and after the tab was selected, with RNGH's own root recogniser on the surface (the view is already in the window, hidden). With the default `lazy` (the pane mounts at its first selection) there was no pane to attach to before the selection, and the handler attached at the first selection left its recogniser on the view. Not verified: that a touch on the selected pane reaches the handler (needs the XCUITest, below).
- **Pop gesture (item 3).** On iOS 26.6 `interactivePopGestureRecognizer` is a `_UIParallaxTransitionPanGestureRecognizer`, a `UIScreenEdgePanGestureRecognizer` subclass (lane G5 below), and its delegate is `SvelteStackView`. `SvelteStackView gestureRecognizer:shouldBeRequiredToFailByGestureRecognizer:` answers YES for an RNGH pan when the recogniser is that one and `gestureRecognizerShouldBegin:` is YES. Verified (the delegate's answers, asked by the probe): pushed screen (two screens) RNGH pan YES, plain `UIPanGestureRecognizer` NO, `shouldBegin` YES; the only screen of the stack RNGH pan NO, `shouldBegin` NO. Touch results: lane G5 below.
- **Sheet drag against a pan inside a sheet (item 4).** Touch results: lane G5 below. What the probe saw: the sheet's own drag is a `UIPanGestureRecognizer` with delegate `_UISheetInteraction` on the `UIDropShadowView` that holds the presented view, an ancestor of the screen view that holds the RNGH recognisers; the `UITransitionView` container has none. Nothing coordinates the two, and nothing needed to (item 4).
- **Lab (iPhone SE 3), `--run checks,quick`.** 28/28 checks, 0 fatals, 0 engine warnings, no new crash report, five runs on the head build: header-8 3.3 to 3.9 % (UIKit's floor), navigation-8 1.0 % / 27 to 30 ms in every run, the others at 0 or 0.4 %. The same three runs on a build with `SvelteStackView`'s delegate and `SvelteEnsureGestureRoot` reverted gave navigation-8 1.0 % / 27 to 28 ms and header-8 3.6 to 3.9 %: the native changes do not move the lab. (iOS wave 4 recorded navigation-8 at 0.00 % / 25 ms on 26 Oct; the 1.0 % is on the branch without these changes too.) **navigation-8 A/B (lane G5, 28 Oct, iOS 26.6, 60 Hz, three runs of `lab --run checks,quick`):** the integration head `feat/svelte-native-next` c75c31491, which has no gesture commit and no react-native-gesture-handler, built on the same Mac checkout (main.jsbundle `af6b7513589b`, the bundle of the final build 86a2de13e), measured navigation-8 at **1.0 % dropped, p95 17 ms, max 27 to 28 ms** in all three runs, header-8 3.8 to 3.9 %, 28/28 checks, 0 fatals. So the gesture branch did not regress it; the figure belongs to the phone and the OS of these days. The repo's own runs agree: the final build 86a2de13e measured 0.9 to 1.0 % / 27 to 29 ms in ten runs on 27 Oct (17:17), and the same commit d1be03de6 gave 1.0 % in one file (11:08) and 0.25 to 0.76 % in the next (11:16): the 0.00 to 0.5 % figures were a good hour of the phone, not a property of a commit. The crash-report pull works with USB up: the run records `crashReports: []` and no `crashReportsError`.

#### iOS status (lane G5, 28 Oct to 1 Nov 2026)

Run on the iPhone SE 3 (iOS 26.6, 60 Hz) with real touches: the XCUITest bundle (`qa/ios-ui-tests`) through `native-qa gestures --platform ios`, over fourteen builds of the Mac checkout. Times are after the touch went down, in the tracer's 16.7 ms frames.

- **The harness, what it took.** The runner builds with `xcodebuild build-for-testing` (team 53P6XV9ZY8) in the GUI Terminal and installs on the phone. To start, the phone must be on USB (`idevice_id -l`; the harness checks and says so), unlocked and awake, with Settings > Developer > Enable UI Automation on; otherwise the runner fails 60 s after `Running tests...` with `Timed out while enabling automation mode`, and the phone writes a sysdiagnose of 46 to 272 MB into its crash log. The test opens the lab's link itself (`XCUIApplication.open`, iOS 16.4+): XCUITest does not observe an app that `devicectl` launched (its state stays not running). Faults found in the shared harness: the Mac's ssh dropped every remote exit status (`login` runs the command; fixed in d254e81c8), which had hidden that the installed app's development profile had expired (`0xe8008011`, a rebuild); the crash-report pull copied those sysdiagnose archives and timed out (now two filtered pulls, `-k -f RasheedSvelteQA` and `-k -f JetsamEvent`); `devicectl` calls made while the runner runs, or just after it, hang for 90 s now and then, so the harness asks the phone nothing while the test runs. The QA app can record what UIKit does (`--trace`, started by `Documents/gesture-trace.on`, which the app turns off as it reads it, or the launch argument `-SvelteQAGestureTrace YES`): every recognizer per frame (state, touches, translation, and its own ended or cancelled with the pan's velocity), a passive recognizer on each window (where each touch landed and what UIKit hit-tested, with its frame), the navigation controllers' depth and UIKit's transition (percent, cancelled, completion velocity) and the sheet's detent and position.
- **Touches first did not reach pushed screens.** The trace showed them landing on `RCTModalHostViewComponentView`, the hidden `<Modal>` of `Confirm` (`Session.svelte`): a screen-sized view that `Modal.svelte` always mounted, where React Native's `Modal.js` renders nothing unless the modal is shown. Fixed in b19c0eef6 (on iOS the host mounts only while shown). Before it every scenario on a pushed screen got no event at all, a tap included; a sheet, presented above, was not affected.
- **Scenarios.** After the fix, in three full runs (12 scenarios, 23 touch actions each): every check of `box`, `box-busy`, `box-js`, `row`, `carousel`, `sheet`, `longpress-tap` (11 checks, among them that an activated long press cancels the `Pressable`'s press), `scroll-native` and `churn` passed in the last two; `box-js` read 6/7 once (a peak of 143 where 144 was needed, since fixed: the finger now goes 10 pt past the commanded distance, a Pan counts from where it activated); `box-js-busy` (recorded) 7/7; `pinch-rotate` 5/5 in the last two (4/5 in the first: rotation 32.5 degrees against 40 ± 5; XCUITest does a pinch and then a rotation, so both-active is not judged); `pushed-edge` below. The harness marks `box`, `box-busy` and `pinch-rotate` `fail` on frames alone (1.4 to 14 % dropped): the runner's accessibility snapshots load the app, so `gestures.frames.*` cannot be judged with XCUITest on iOS.
- **Pop (item 3).** The edge swipe is a `UIScreenEdgePanGestureRecognizer` subclass (edges left, layout direction left to right). Zone, drags at 150 pt/s: from x = 1, 2 and 3 pt it began (+174 to +178 ms); from 4, 6, 8, 10 and 12 it failed at touch-down; from 16 to 100 it never saw the touch; the same with UIKit's own delegate. A drag that starts without the 50 ms press (a fling), tried once, was failed at touch-down even from 2 pt. From x = 2 pt with the lab's swipe (plain press and drag, 300 pt, three runs on build 11): the swipe began at +66.5, +86.1 and +81 ms, the screen popped (depth 2 to 1) and the RNGH Pan was failed in that frame (in one run its recogniser had reached Began at +63.9 ms and UIKit held it until the swipe decided: the lab counted no onStart), so `edge swipe popped the screen` and `the pan never started` hold; UIKit completed the pop at 68 to 69 % with the finger at 697 to 734 pt/s. From x = 100 pt (every run) the swipe failed 0.3 to 0.7 ms after touch-down, the Pan began at +83 ms, in the first frame its translation passed 10 pt (the frame before: 4.5 to 10 pt), and the screen stayed. **No added wait:** the Pan began at +83 to +101 ms on a pushed screen (four box runs) and at +81 ms on a screen at the root of the stack (one run, the page opened by link), within one frame. **Release:** the pop is UIKit's stock interactive transition (`SvelteStackView` and `SvelteNavigationController` have no interaction controller or animator, and react to a cancel only by restoring the bars) and UIKit decides on release: with the finger moving at lift it completed at 68 to 69 %; in two earlier runs of the plain drag (205 and 289 pt of 375, 55 and 77 %) it cancelled after the finger let go; released at rest (a 0.4 s hold) it cancelled at 73 % and completed at 79 %. " + CONTROL + " The check `edge swipe popped the screen` therefore failed in 2 of the 6 plain-drag runs from 2 pt, whatever the arbitration did: it held in every run.
- **Sheet (item 4).** Build 10, the lab's four drags: the grabber: the sheet's drag began at +66.5 ms, detent 0 to 1, the sheet moved 326 pt; the ScrollView at the top detent: the scroll view's pan began at +83.2 ms and the sheet's recogniser failed in that frame (the sheet moved 4 pt, the list scrolled 448 px); a default Pan down: the RNGH pan began at +66.5 ms and the sheet's recogniser failed in that frame (2 pt); a Pan with `activeOffsetY([-4, 4])` up: began at +83.1 ms and the sheet's recogniser failed in that frame (3 pt), the detent unchanged. In each case the first recogniser to activate won and the other failed at that moment. The default Pan won where Android's sheet wins; both thresholds were crossed in one frame, so the order of a tie is not a rule.
- **Tab pane (item 1).** A handler on a pane that was not selected is bound (probe, 28 Oct, above). Not verified with a touch.
- **Lab (iPhone SE 3), `--run checks,quick`, the final build 14 (branch head b4ed53c24, recording off):** 28/28 checks in the three runs, 0 fatals, 0 engine warnings, no crash report (the filtered pull works with the phone on USB). No frame regression against c75c31491 (three runs of the same day): navigation-8 1.0 % / 28 to 29 ms (1.0 % there too), header-8 3.2 to 3.8 % (3.8 to 3.9 % there), tabs-8 0.0 % / 24 to 25 ms (0.0 % there), every other scenario 0.0 %, except one frame of 133 ms in `sheet-8` of one run (0.1 %). Two labs earlier on builds 10 and 13 read tabs-8 0.4 to 0.8 % in 8 of 9 runs, and that was the recording: the QA app's flag file `Documents/gesture-trace.on`, which `--trace` runs set to 1, stayed on and the app sampled the view tree every frame under those labs (the flag now turns itself off as it is read). Lesson for the numbers above: a lab is measured with the recording off.
- **Review fixes (1 Nov 2026), iOS: written on Linux, not compiled, not run: they need the Mac.** (1) `SvelteEnsureGestureRoot` and the RNGH patch (item 2 above): the patch gains `+currentManager` and `-registerRootViewGestureRecognizerIfAttachedBelow:`, `SvelteSupport.mm` calls them through a protocol declared in the file, `SvelteGestureRootDelegate` and the subtree walk are gone. To check on the phone: a pushed page and a sheet whose handlers attach before the screen has its place still get the root recogniser (now `delegate` = `RNGestureHandlerManager`), `longpress-tap` still cancels the `Pressable` when the long press activates, a JS responder that answers `true` on grant makes the root recogniser wait (`handleSetJSResponder`), and the lab's `[Svelte boot]` of an app without a gesture is unchanged. (2) `Modal.svelte`: UIKit reports `onDismiss` only for a controller it presented (`RCTModalHostViewComponentView`: `_isPresented`, which needs a window), so a `<Modal>` toggled true to false while off-window (a hidden eager tab pane) stayed mounted hidden and swallowed that pane's touches. `onShow` now says the controller was presented: the host leaves with `visible` unless `onShow` came since `visible` turned true (`tests/screens-ios.test.ts` on the host; the same toggle in a hidden tab pane on the phone). (3) `SvelteQA` is no longer linked into the product build (see "QA-only modules"); the QA variant's Podfile excludes the smoke test's libraries only.
- **Not verified.** A person's finger: the edge zone and the release of a swipe are measured with XCUITest's synthesized touches only. A pinch and a rotation at the same time (XCUITest does one after the other). A touch on a gesture in a tab pane. `enabled(false)` through the root recogniser, RTL, Hover. The long run (`gestures --repeat 10`, about an hour; offered, not launched). Frames under a touch (the runner's snapshots perturb them).

### Coexistence rules (both platforms unless marked)

| Situation | Behaviour | How it holds |
|---|---|---|
| Pan on a row inside a vertical `ScrollView` (`activeOffsetX`, `failOffsetY`) | Horizontal drag: the pan activates and the scroll never starts; vertical drag: the pan fails and the list scrolls | Android: root swallows after activation; the scroll view's `requestDisallow` cancels handlers. iOS: RNGH's own scroll-view compatibility. |
| Pan carousel (horizontal) inside a vertical scroll view | The dominant axis wins | Same offsets. |
| `Pressable` under a gesture view | Press and Tap both fire for a tap; a pan that activates cancels the press (`onPressOut`, no `onPress`) | Android step 5.3; iOS the root recogniser. |
| Android edge swipe back | Native wins ties inside the 24 dp zone: a touch that starts there and moves a touch slop to the right is the stack's, unless a handler activated on an earlier sample. A vertical drag from the edge reaches a vertical pan | Android: the claim (item 5.3) asks `EngineStack` before the helper sees the move; the stack's `startDrag` takes the lock, which cancels the handlers. System gesture navigation never reaches the app: the system cancels the touch (document `setSystemGestureExclusionRects` as a follow-up if a carousel must own the edge). iOS: item 3 (the zone is UIKit's, 1 to 3 pt for synthesized touches; the edge swipe wins it, a Pan elsewhere, with no added wait). |
| Android sheet drag vs a pan inside the sheet | Ties go to the sheet, as for the edge: `EngineSheet` claims at a touch slop (8 dp), and a default Pan's `minDist` is that same `getScaledTouchSlop` (`PanGestureHandler.defaultMinDist`; not 10 dp), so they cross on one sample and the sheet is asked first. A pan with `activeOffsetY([-4, 4])` activates on an earlier sample and wins | Android: the claim (item 5.3), same distance rule as RNGH's default pan (`GestureRules.sheetDrag`, host-checked against `dx * dx + dy * dy >= slop * slop`). Checked by the lab's two variants with both drivers. iOS: item 4 (measured: the first recogniser to activate wins and the other fails then; a default Pan won the tie). |
| `Pan.minDistance(n)` with `n` larger than the touch slop | Never beats the stack's edge swipe or a sheet's drag: both claim at the slop, the pan would activate at `n` | Android: `GestureRules` claim distance (host-checked against RNGH's `shouldActivate`). iOS: the first recogniser to activate wins (item 4 below), and UIKit's activates at its own slop, before a pan that needs more distance. Use `activeOffsetX`/`activeOffsetY` smaller than the slop to win the tie. |
| A JS responder that blocks the native ones (`onResponderGrant` answers `true`) | iOS: RNGH's root recognisers wait (`handleSetJSResponder` -> `blockOtherRecognizers`), as with React Native's `UIManager.setJSResponder`. Android: nothing, for an engine root | The responder (`fabric/responder.ts`) tells RNGH's module on grant (`blockNativeResponder`) and on release (`releaseNativeResponder`) once the gesture host is loaded; RNGH patches `UIManager.setJSResponder` in a file the host cannot load. Android: `RNGestureHandlerModule.handleSetJSResponder` finds its root through `resolveRootTagFromReactTag`, which Fabric answers 0 for a node tag, and requires a `ReactRootView`; an engine root is neither, so the call does nothing (React Native's own roots have the same limit upstream). `Pressability`'s default grant answers nothing, so only a user's `onResponderGrant` returning `true` reaches it. |
| Foreign views (WebView, map) | Inherit the platform rules; a gesture can be attached to them (React Native's view carries the tag) | `EngineForeign` is a plain `ViewGroup` to RNGH. |
| Modal layers | Gestures below a modal never start | Item 3 (Android) and the layer being above in z (iOS). |

### Test plan

Without a device (all `bun test` in `packages/svelte-native`, in `tests/gestures/`):
1. **Golden vectors.** `tests/gestures/golden/rngh-2.32.0-native-calls.json` holds, for twelve gesture shapes, the exact native call sequence of RNGH's own `attachHandlers`, `updateHandlers` and `dropHandlers` (`createGestureHandler`, `attachGestureHandler`, `updateGestureHandler` with `waitFor`/`simultaneousHandlers`/`blocksHandlers`, `dropGestureHandler`, `flushOperations`). `node tests/gestures/capture-golden.mjs` regenerates it; run it after any RNGH upgrade and read the diff. The Svelte detector, driven through a compiled fixture with `<View gesture={...}>` and a fake `TurboModuleRegistry` recorder, must produce the same list after normalising handler tags to first-seen order and the view tag to the node's tag. Facts the vectors pin: `pan-js-offsets` (action type 4 with offset keys), `pan-worklet` and `pan-no-callbacks-counts-as-worklet` (type 1), `pan-mixed-runOnJS` (a `runOnJS(true)` gesture with worklet callbacks is type 4; `hitSlop` object, `enabled`, `manualActivation`, `shouldCancelWhenOutside` sent as given), `hitslop-number` (the number goes through as a number), `simultaneous-pan-pinch-rotation` (`simultaneousHandlers: [5,6]`, `[4,6]`, `[4,5]`), `exclusive-doubletap-singletap` (the second tap has `waitFor: [7]`), `race-longpress-tap` (no relations), `external-relations-native-then-pan` (`waitFor`, `simultaneousHandlers`, `blocksHandlers` all `[13]`), `update-same-shape` (a new object of the same shape sends only `updateGestureHandler` with the new config and `flushOperations`). The capture script marks callbacks as worklets by setting `__workletHash` on one shared function, which stays set for every vector after `pan-worklet`: from `hitslop-number` on, the callbacks count as worklets and the action type is 1 whatever the shape's name suggests. `golden.test.ts` repeats the sequence to compare the vectors as they are; JS-thread (type 4) gestures are covered by `lifecycle.test.ts` and `events.test.ts`.
2. **Lifecycle** with the fake module: mount, update, destroy and two HMR remounts give no call after a detach, one `dropGestureHandler` per handler, fresh tags; a changed `handlerName`, count or thread reattaches (drop then create, event handler rebuilt); a relation to a gesture that mounts later updates the referencing handler exactly once; one `flushOperations` per batch; a gesture shared by two nodes throws RNGH's message.
3. **Event routing.** Feed state, update and touch event sequences to the fake `RCTDeviceEventEmitter` and compare callback order and arguments with RNGH's own `onGestureHandlerEvent` (bundled as the oracle, as the capture script bundles `attachHandlers`). Run the ported UI worklet on the JS thread with the same sequences (`State.UNDETERMINED -> BEGAN -> ACTIVE -> END` and the failure and cancel paths, `onChange` through `changeEventCalculator`, `isWorklet[type] === false` warning).
4. **Fake Reanimated.** Extend `tests/reanimated-stubs.ts` with `WorkletEventHandler` (records `registerForEvents(tag)`, `updateEventHandler`, `unregisterFromEvents(tag)`) and `makeMutable`: assert one registration per detector for the node's tag, none for JS-only gestures, `sharedHandlers.value` set and cleared.
5. **Toolchain.** Run `transformer.ts` on a `.svelte` fixture and assert the inline `Gesture.Pan().onUpdate(...)` callback gets `__workletHash` and a closure of exactly its captured SharedValues; assert the `$state`-reading fixture produces the development error naming the variable; assert `import { Gesture as G }` produces the "none of the callbacks are worklets" warning.
6. **Import isolation.** esbuild-bundle `src/gestures/index.ts` and `src/fabric/gestures.ts` with a metafile: no `createHandler`, no RNGH `index.ts` or `components/`, no `reanimatedWrapper`, no `react-native` import except through `deps-rn`; importing the entry does not evaluate Reanimated (the stub's side effect counter stays 0 until the first `Gesture` read). Also: the entry adds nothing to a bundle that never imports it (`host.ts` loads `fabric/gestures.ts` only through `require` on the first gesture node).
7. **Types.** `svelte-check` on a fixture using every builder method and `gesture` on each primitive.
8. **Android host checks** (no device): `android/src/test` pure-Kotlin check for the pointer-events mapping and the id rules (`kotlinc`, the style of `AnimatedWireCheck.kt`); `:svelte-engine:compileReleaseKotlin`, `:app:assembleRelease`.

**Status, 28 Oct 2026 (lane G1).** Items 1 to 7 are `packages/svelte-native/tests/gestures/`: `golden` (1), `lifecycle` and `primitives` (2), `events` (3: the JS receiver and the ported UI worklet against RNGH's `onGestureHandlerEvent` on seven event sequences), the fake Reanimated in `lifecycle` and `events` (4), `toolchain` (5: the real `transformer.ts` over `.svelte` fixtures; the plugin workletizes the inline callbacks and the closure of one is exactly its captures; `$state` and `$props` reads are refused naming the variable; `import { Gesture as G }` gets the "none of the callbacks are worklets" warning), `isolation` (6: an esbuild metafile; RNGH's 22 pure modules and nothing else of it, `react-native` only through `deps-rn.ts`, nothing of the host reachable through an ES `import` from `host.ts`, `index.ts`, `fabric/index.ts` or `animation.ts`, and the entry's only RNGH imports at start are the five constants) and `types` (7: `svelte-check` over every builder method and `gesture` on each primitive, with the wrong uses expected to fail), plus `config`, `guards` and `entry`. They run on the host: **nothing here was run on a device, emulator or Mac.** The `[Svelte boot]` of an app that never uses gestures is unchanged by construction (the host is a lazy `require`, the entry loads no builder and no Reanimated; `isolation.test.ts` asserts both and that no `gestures` phase appears in the summary), not by measurement on a phone.

On a device, in the lab. New route `src/routes/__dev/gestures/+mobile.svelte` (`?mode=`), state and counters in `src/lib/native/dev/gestures-lab.ts`, and stress scenarios `gestures-<mode>` in `Stress.svelte`, outside `quick` (run with `--run gestures-box,...`). Each scenario opens its page, waits for layout, logs `[Svelte gesture] ready {"mode","rects":{name:{x,y,w,h}}}` in pixels (from `measureInWindow` times the density) so the harness can aim, arms the existing UI-runtime frame monitor (`frames.ts`), waits for the gesture's end callbacks or 20 s, stops the monitor, and returns counters, frames and a verdict like the other scenarios (`[Svelte stress]` lines, so `lab-run.ts` already parses them):

| Scenario | Content | Input | Pass |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gestures-box` | A draggable `AnimatedView` (Pan on the UI thread, `onEnd` springs back). Variants `?js=busy` (the JS thread blocks 40 ms of every 50) and `?mode=box-js` (`runOnJS(true)`). | one drag, 400 ms, 200 x 120 px | begin, start, end(success), finalize once; update at least 10; box rests at 0 after the spring; frames smooth (at most 1 % dropped, none over 4 periods). **`?js=busy` stays smooth with the same counters** (the UI-thread proof); `box-js` under busy JS is recorded in `observed`, not judged. |
| `gestures-pinch-rotate` | `Simultaneous(Pinch, Rotation)` on an `AnimatedView` (scale, rotation) | two pointers, 3x spread and 40 degrees, 600 ms | both handlers active together; final scale within 5 % and rotation within 3 degrees of the injected values; smooth. |
| `gestures-row` | 20 swipe-to-delete rows in a vertical `ScrollView` (`activeOffsetX([-10,10])`, `failOffsetY([-10,10])`; past a threshold the row is removed) | swipe row 3 left; then swipe row 5 up | row 3 removed, scroll offset unchanged (1 px); row 5 untouched (translation 0, pan `finalize(false)`), list scrolled more than 100 px. |
| `gestures-carousel` | Horizontal Pan pager (snap) inside a vertical `ScrollView` | horizontal, vertical and 30 degree diagonal swipes | page changes on the horizontal, scroll on the vertical, the dominant axis on the diagonal; no double handling (counters). |
| `gestures-pushed-edge` | A pushed screen with a full-width horizontal Pan box | swipe from x = 8 dp; swipe from x = 100 dp | Android: the first pops the screen and the pan never starts (and RNGH handlers are cancelled: `finalize(false)`); the second starts the pan and does not pop. iOS: same policy from x = 2 pt, UIKit's zone (item 3 of the iOS contract). |
| `gestures-sheet` | A two-detent sheet with a vertical Pan box with default offsets, one with `activeOffsetY([-4,4])`, and a scroll view | drags on the grabber, each box and the scroll | grabber: detent changes; default box: sheet wins (Android; iOS recorded); `[-4,4]` box: pan wins and the detent is unchanged; scroll scrolls. |
| `gestures-longpress-tap` | `Exclusive(LongPress(500 ms), Tap)` and a `Race` variant, with a `Pressable` inside | tap; 700 ms hold; hold with 15 px drift | tap: tap fires once, long press `finalize(false)`; hold: long press once, tap cancelled; drift: both end failed; the Pressable's press and the gesture agree with "Coexistence". |
| `gestures-scroll-native` | `Pan` with `simultaneousWithExternalGesture(Gesture.Native())` on a `ScrollView` | vertical drag | both active (counters); the scroll offset moves. |
| `gestures-churn` | 200 mounts and unmounts of a view with three gestures, half UI half JS | none | no handler left (`gestures.stats()` from the host module: records, handlers, event handlers all 0), `engine.stats().alive` back to baseline, no native error in the log. |

Gates (`qa/gates.json`, new group `gestures`): every scenario above passes its checks, `gestures-box` and `gestures-pinch-rotate` frames within the existing smooth bar, `gestures-churn` leaks 0. The engine writes `SvelteEngine.gesture` trace slices, so a Perfetto capture of `gestures-box` attributes main-thread time per touch event.

Driving the lab:
- **Android, single pointer: `adb shell input swipe x1 y1 x2 y2 <ms>`** (a zero-length swipe of 700 ms is a long press; `input tap`; `input motionevent DOWN|MOVE|UP x y` for stepped drags, API 31+), through the real input pipeline. `input swipe` injects MOVEs as fast as the shell can, not frame-paced, so it proves counters, not latency.
- **Android, multi-pointer and frame-paced: an in-app injector**, `SvelteQaTouch.run(json)` (`modules/svelte-qa-android`, `QaTouch.kt`), an Expo module of its own that the product build leaves out of autolinking (`autolinkingExclude` in `app.config.ts`, see "QA-only modules" below) and that refuses to run outside the QA variant (`io.rasheed.svelteqa`) should it be linked: a list of per-pointer paths, one `MotionEvent.obtain(downTime, eventTime, action, pointerCount, properties, coords, ...)` per `Choreographer` frame with `eventTime` = the frame time, dispatched with `EngineRoot.dispatchTouchEvent` (public API, same process, no instrumentation, exact coordinates). It skips `InputDispatcher` and `ViewRootImpl`, so every single-pointer scenario also runs once through `adb input` and the counters must agree. `sendevent` on the emulator's virtual touchscreen is the end-to-end fallback for pinch and is slower (one process per event).
- **iOS: touches cannot be synthesized from inside the app without private API, so none is attempted.** The cheapest honest paths: on a **simulator**, AXe's `touch --down/--up` and `swipe` for single pointer and the simulator HID tools (Argent's `gesture-custom`, `gesture-pinch`, `gesture-rotate`) for two pointers, which feed real UIKit recognisers (functional results; simulator frame times are not representative); on the **iPhone SE 3**, a small XCUITest bundle (`qa/ios-ui-tests/`, a standalone project whose runner targets the installed app by bundle id; the lab boxes carry `testID`s, so `XCUIApplication.otherElements["gesture-box"]` is found by accessibility identifier and no pixel handshake is needed) using `XCUICoordinate.press(forDuration:thenDragTo:withVelocity:thenHoldForDuration:)`, `pinch(withScale:velocity:)` and `rotate(_:withVelocity:)`, run with `xcodebuild test-without-building` over `ssh` to the Mac while the app logs `[Svelte stress]` as it does today. The bundle exists and ran: see "iOS status (lane G5)" for what it took (the phone on USB, unlocked, with UI Automation enabled) and for the results.
- **Frame drops during a gesture-driven animation:** the lab's UI-runtime monitor (`startFrameMonitor`/`stopFrameMonitor`) around the injected gesture, same bar as `churn-animated`, p50/p95/p99/max recorded; on Android also `dumpsys gfxinfo` janky-frame counts and the trace slices; on iOS the same in-app monitor (it runs on the UI runtime there too).

### QA-only modules

Code that only the QA lab needs does not ship. `app.config.ts` passes `autolinkingExclude` to the svelte-native plugin, the one list the native module smoke test's libraries use too (`smokeOnlyPackages`, "Native modules (smoke test)"): `svelte-qa-android` (the touch injector), `svelte-qa-ios` (`SvelteQA`, `SvelteQAGestureTrace`, the boot recorder) and `view-bench` when `APP_VARIANT=production`, on top of the smoke-only packages (the product and the QA app leave those out, the smoke app links them); the QA app and the smoke app keep the QA modules, and the smoke app excludes nothing. The plugin writes the names into `settings.gradle` (`expoAutolinking.exclude`) and the Podfile (`use_expo_modules!(exclude: svelte_native_exclude)`) on every prebuild, so a directory can be prebuilt for one variant and then the other. Each is an Expo module of its own for that reason (the injector used to be listed beside `SvelteEngineModule` in the engine's module config, which autolinking cannot split). Each still refuses to run outside the QA bundle if it is linked. Proof: `apps/platform/qa/test/product-modules.test.ts` resolves the module list with the autolinking CLI and the configured exclusion; a product prebuild (`APP_VARIANT=production expo prebuild --platform android`) followed by `:expo:generatePackagesList` lists `SvelteEngineModule` and `SvelteUpdatesModule` and nothing QA (1 Nov 2026). The engine's lab switches (`LabSwitches.kt`) are read only by the QA app and debuggable builds.


### What the product contains: no development lab (JavaScript)

The lab (`/__dev/**`, `src/routes/__dev`, the modules of `src/lib/native/dev`, the lab's image, and the libraries only they import) is in the QA app and the smoke app and not in the product (`APP_VARIANT=production`, `io.rasheed.app`, EAS `preview` and `production`). Four layers, from the source to the backstop:

1. **Route discovery.** `routeFilesSync` (`packages/svelte-native/src/build.ts`), which `svelte-native sync`, the prebuild plugin and the Metro config run, does not read a `__dev` directory (at any depth) when `labIncluded()` is false (the variant is `production`), and leaves out a route whose module script exports `lab = true` (a literal, like `presentation` and `navigator`): a page alone, a layout with its whole directory (the root layout, which is the shell, cannot). `lab` in the instance script is an error (`move lab to <script module>`), not silence. The route tree, the generated table and so Metro's module graph never name the lab. `syncNative(root, { variant, lab })` takes the choice explicitly. Every other variant discovers it, so the QA matrix, the smoke test and `svelte-native dev` are unchanged.
2. **The bundle says what it carries, and the app follows it.** Each variant has a route table of its own, `.svelte-native/routes.<variant>.ts` (`routes.production.ts`, `routes.qa.ts`, `routes.smoke.ts`); there is no table the variants share. It exports `variant`, `lab` and `tree`. The Metro integration (`withSvelteNative`) writes its variant's table when the config loads, before Metro's file map is built (and in development, `NODE_ENV=development`, again whenever a file under `src/routes` changes), and resolves two specifiers to it: `./routes`, imported by the generated `.svelte-native/entry.ts`, and `@rasheed/svelte-native/routes`, which the app reads. `state.svelte.ts` derives `nativeLabEnabled` from that table's `lab` (`labDecision`, `lab-flag.ts`): the lab runs only when the bundle has it, and then only in an app that may run it (a development run, the QA or the smoke app; never `io.rasheed.app`, whatever `__DEV__` says). So a QA dev client with a product-variant Metro runs no lab although its id would allow it, and a product shell sent a bundle that carries the lab (an update made without `APP_VARIANT=production`) logs `[Svelte lab] this qa bundle carries the development lab, which the io.rasheed.app app does not run …` and runs none of it. The three application ids are one module (`src/lib/native/app-ids.ts`), read by `app.config.ts`, the QA harness, the operator app registry and the flag.
3. **Shared code.** Three screens (login, workspaces, the workspace home) show `<LabLink />` and the session reads the launch link through `launchedIntoLab()`, both behind `nativeLabEnabled`; the root layout installs `errors.ts`, hands `qa.ts` the boot phases, and reports the running bundle and plants the late faults of the update harness (`update-probe.ts`) behind the same flag. All of them are modules of `src/lib/native/dev`, so the product's shared code holds only the calls. `app-tree.test.ts` mounts the real root layout, session and screens with the lab's directories replaced by the product's own stub files and the lab off, so a lab use that is not behind the flag fails the suite. The stub throws on a *read* of a binding, not only on a call, because Metro's transform reads a named import where it is used (`_probe.lateFault`) while esbuild copies a CommonJS module's exports into a namespace when it imports it, and the stub has none to copy: an earlier version of the test let `lateFault === "mount"` read `undefined` and an unguarded read of it crashed the product's root layout unseen. Each import of a stubbed directory now goes through a module that lists the bindings the importer names (`ownKeys`), so esbuild's copy reads them through the stub's `get`, which throws `src/lib/native/dev/ is not in this app (APP_VARIANT=production)` where the binding is used; the test fails on an unguarded `lateFault` read in the layout and on an unguarded `launchedIntoLab()` in the session (both tried and reverted on 1 Nov 2026), and `lab-stub-probe.svelte` keeps a read, a call and a component of the lab as permanent proof that the net sees each. The bundle is still not the product: `native-qa product` (qa/README.md, "product") installs the product built with `APP_VARIANT=production` on the OVH emulator, starts it cold and passes only if it reaches the login screen within 15 s with no JavaScript fatal and no native crash.
4. **Metro backstop.** `metro.config.cjs` gives the product `stubbedDirectories` for `src/lib/native/dev` and `src/routes/__dev`: every import that resolves into one, by alias or by path, gets the directory's stub (`.svelte-native/stubs/production/src_lib_native_dev_.js`: importing it is harmless, using it throws `src/lib/native/dev/ is not in this app (APP_VARIANT=production)`). The product bundle holds the stub for the layout's imports of the lab and nothing else of it. The stubs are a directory per variant (they name it in their message) and a stub that is current is not written again.

**What is guaranteed.** The variants share no generated file but the entry, which does not depend on the variant. A sync, a bundle or a Metro of one variant never writes a file another variant's Metro reads, so a running QA development server, its warm graph included, is not changed by a product bundle or a sync for any variant (`bundle.test.ts` checks the modification times of the QA and smoke tables, the entry and every stub across product, smoke and QA bundles). A bundle's route table is the table of its own variant: a forgotten `svelte-native sync` before `eas update`, or a QA sync followed by a product export, changes nothing (`bundle.test.ts` syncs for the QA app and bundles the product: no lab route, a bundle byte-identical to the one without the sync). The entry is written by `svelte-native sync` and the prebuild plugin as `SVELTE_NATIVE_ROOT` has it, and by the Metro integration only when it is missing or when its environment names a root, so a Metro that starts without `SVELTE_NATIVE_ROOT` does not revert the entry of a spike root. **What no check can know** is the variant itself: publish an update with `APP_VARIANT=production` in the environment (the EAS `preview` and `production` environments need it, as the build profiles in `eas.json` have it); an update made without it is a QA-variant bundle, and what the product shell does with that is the refusal above.

Defence in depth stays: `enforceSession` (`session-guard.ts`) sends a path under `/__dev` to the workspaces (signed in) or the login when the build has no lab (`nativeLabEnabled` is false). In the product a link `rasheed://__dev` is not found (`openURL` ignores `/__dev/screens`, `/__dev/tree/inbox`, … while the app runs), or reads as a workspace slug `__dev` that is not the user's (`/__dev` alone, any single segment names a workspace), which the guard turns into the list; `packages/svelte-native/tests/app-tree.test.ts` drives both on the app's real route files for the product and for the lab.

What the product's bundle lost (`expo export:embed`, release, not minified, the recipe of `qa/test/bundle.test.ts`): 11,738,008 bytes and 1,345 modules before, 10,760,249 bytes and 1,188 after (-977,759 bytes, -8.3 %). The QA app's 11,738,000 became 11,740,497 (+2,497: `LabLink.svelte` and `launch.ts`, the two modules the hooks moved into) and the smoke app's 14,802,752 became 14,805,249. The 160 modules only the QA app has are the lab's 52, 82 of `packages/svelte-native` that only the lab imports (the foreign view wrappers, the svg components, the animation and gesture bridges), 24 of react-native-svg, expo-video and expo-keep-awake, the lab's image and the smoke checks' stub. The product's `__dev` text is the one line of the session guard. Hermes bytecode of `index.android.bundle` in the x86_64 release APK (`native-qa build` for the QA app, a product prebuild with `APP_VARIANT=production` and `:app:assembleRelease`, both green on 1 Nov 2026): the QA app 5,733,356 bytes, the product 5,050,024 (-683,332, -11.9 %); the product prebuild's `routes.production.ts` names no `__dev` file and the APK's bytecode holds `__dev` once, in the guard.

`bundle.test.ts` (about a minute: it makes nine bundles) lists the modules of each bundle from its source map: the product has no module under `src/lib/native/dev` or `src/routes/__dev` (only the directory's stub and the shared stub core), no `Mobile development lab` text and no line naming `__dev` but the guard's; the QA app has every route of the lab and its hooks; every module of the product is in the QA app, the modules only the QA app has are the lab's or a dependency only it imports, and the smoke app has the QA app's. A route that must stay out of the product wherever it lives sets `export const lab = true`; a module outside `dev/` that only the lab needs goes into `dev/` and is imported behind `nativeLabEnabled`.

### Lanes

All lanes start from this section. G1, G2 and G3 can start together: they meet only at three agreed names (`SvelteEngine.enableGestures()`, the `gesture` attribute, the golden call sequences). G4 starts its pages once G1's `Gesture` and attribute compile (day 1) and finishes last, because it needs devices.

| Lane | Owns | Depends on | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| **G1 JS API and tests** | `packages/svelte-native/{src/gestures/**, src/fabric/gestures.ts, src/components/GestureDetector.svelte, src/fabric/host.ts (three call sites), src/fabric/reanimated-internals.*, src/animation.ts, src/lower.ts, src/elements.d.ts, component prop types, package.json exports, tests/gestures/**, README.md}` | none | Test plan items 1 to 7 green in `bun test`; golden vectors match; `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` clean; boot cost of an app that never uses gestures unchanged (the entry is lazy: `[Svelte boot]` total equal within noise); every developer guard (closure capture, mixed worklets, shared gesture, `collapsable`) has a test; README section written. |
| **G2 Android engine** | `apps/platform/modules/svelte-engine/android/**`, `app.config.ts` (on-demand package), this SPEC section's Android status | none (names agreed above) | Items 1 to 10 of the Android contract implemented; `:svelte-engine:compileReleaseKotlin` and `:app:assembleRelease` compile; host checks of the pointer-events mapping and ids pass; the existing lab (28 checks) and `parity` (the 200 Android references) unchanged at 0 px with gestures never enabled and with `enableGestures()` called; on the OVH emulator, a gesture on a plain view reaches a JS callback and a worklet callback (the lab's `gestures-box` counters). |
| **G3 iOS** | `apps/platform/modules/svelte-screens-ios/**`, `patches/react-native-gesture-handler@2.32.0.patch` (and its `patch_hash` in `pnpm-lock.yaml`), `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (`patchedDependencies`), `apps/platform/modules/svelte-qa-ios/**` (the probe) | none | The patch applies (`pnpm install --prefer-offline --ignore-scripts`); the pods build with RNGH; `SvelteStackView` delegate and `SvelteTouchHost` behaviour implemented; on the device or simulator, by the probe: the root recogniser exists on the surface and in a sheet, a handler attached after mount is bound, and the pop-gesture and sheet items of the iOS contract are each answered "verified: ..." or "not verified: ..." in this SPEC. |
| **G4 lab and harness** | `apps/platform/src/lib/native/dev/gestures-lab.ts`, `src/routes/__dev/gestures/+mobile.svelte`, `Stress.svelte` entries, `apps/platform/qa/{commands/gestures.ts, lib/inject-android.ts, lib/iphone/gestures.ts, ios-ui-tests/**, gates.json, README.md}`, `apps/platform/modules/svelte-qa-android/**` | G1 (attribute), G2 and G3 for runs | The nine scenarios exist and pass on the emulator (Android) and, as far as the iOS driver exists, on the device; frames recorded for `gestures-box` and `gestures-pinch-rotate` on the A16 (90 Hz) and the iPhone SE 3; the harness handshake (`ready` rects, `input swipe`, `qaGesture`) documented in `qa/README.md`; `gestures` gates wired into `native-qa gates`; the long run (`lab --repeat 10` with the gestures scenarios, about an hour) is offered, not launched. |

### Risks and what could not be determined from reading

- **Engine ids and context.** Setting `View.id` and a `ThemedReactContext` on every engine view is a broad change to code that is parity-tested to 0 px (200 Android references; the a11y oracle reads `resource-id`). Look at `qa/lib/uia.ts` (how `resource-id` is parsed) and run `parity` and `a11y` before and after. If the context change is unwelcome, the alternative is a patch to RNGH's event classes that takes the surface id from the root helper; the ids cannot be avoided (events and Reanimated key on `view.id`).
- **Worklet serialisation of a Svelte closure.** What Reanimated does when handed `{ $, count }` (a module namespace and a signal object) was not run: it may throw, or clone a stale snapshot silently. The development guard makes the outcome irrelevant for the supported path; to see the failure, read `react-native-worklets/src/memory/serializable.ts` (`createSerializable`) and try the fixture in a lab page. In a release build the guard is off (it reads `__closure`, which exists in release too: decide whether it should stay on).
- **Android arbitration races.** Resolved by the claim for the two native drags (item 5.3): they and a default Pan share `getScaledTouchSlop`, so what decides is the order inside one sample, not the numbers of a device. What remains is RNGH's own: `PanGestureHandler.onHandle` checks `shouldFail` before `shouldActivate`, so a move that carries the finger past a `failOffset` and an `activeOffset` together fails the pan (a 30 degree flick with `activeOffsetX(10)`, `failOffsetY(10)` needs one event with 10 to 17.3 dp across; the lab's diagonal takes 600 ms for that reason). `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper.passingTouch` ignores `requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent` calls made while the orchestrator delivers events (a `Gesture.Native()` scroll view being fed): read `RNGestureHandlerRootHelper.kt:104-121` if a native scroll inside a simultaneous pan misbehaves.
- **Children of an intercepting root get no cancel** (React Native parity): a foreign view (WebView, map) that was mid-touch when a pan activated sees no `ACTION_CANCEL` until the next DOWN. A synthetic cancel through `super.dispatchTouchEvent` would fix it but breaks a `Gesture.Native()` scroll view that RNGH keeps feeding; not done.
- **iOS arbitration** (pop gesture, sheet drag, a pan next to `UIScrollView` inside a sheet) is not determinable from source and is the largest unknown; the verification list in the iOS contract is the plan. `RNGestureHandlerModule.mm` runs its methods on `RCTGetUIManagerQueue()`: confirm the queue runs in bridgeless with this app's `init.ts` (RNGH 2.x works in stock bridgeless apps; the difference here is the custom init).
- **Build.** RNGH's `libgesturehandler.so` (CMake, `-Werror`, `REACT_NATIVE_MINOR_VERSION >= 81` branch of `cpp-adapter.cpp`) and its Kotlin sources build against RN 0.86.3 (`:app:assembleRelease` for the QA variant, x86_64, 0 compiler errors, 1 Nov 2026). The lockfile change that added the dependency also flipped `optional: true` off for four transitive packages (`@egjs/hammerjs`, `@types/hammerjs`, `@types/react-test-renderer`, `hoist-non-react-statics`): a clean `pnpm install` is the check.
- **Not covered.** Accessibility (TalkBack touch exploration bypasses gestures; RNGH has a special case in its button only), Hover and trackpad on a device with a pointer, ForceTouch, RTL mirroring of `activeOffsetX` (RNGH does not mirror; the app must), Android system back gesture exclusion, `Animated.event` and the legacy API.
